# Source provenance

Title: CSS Fonts Module Level 4

Source snapshot: https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/

Snapshot SHA-256: 03626a0c85658aaa94191f1b8c64dd5491b723acff08cc33ddd0331a80b920f5

Conversion: offline format conversion of the exact stored HTML; not a new specification or summary. Publication versions remain distinct. Source fragment identifiers are preserved as short HTML anchors. Original copyright and licensing text/links are retained where present in the source.

Representation notes:
- The 57 source tables are presented as readable Markdown tables or explicit labeled layouts: 52 ordinary table conversions, 5 already-readable tables. Source cell content, links and relationships are retained.
- Added table headings and layout labels are non-normative presentation aids. Source header/data roles and span models remain in the conversion checks; GFM cannot reproduce native HTML th/scope/rowspan/colspan accessibility semantics. Source row-header labels are bold where used in ordinary Markdown tables.
- Small semantic emphasis/subscript/superscript HTML is retained to avoid GFM intraword-delimiter and subscript rendering defects; website layout HTML is not retained.
- Canonically unstable or combining Unicode characters and escape-sensitive punctuation are shielded as numeric entities in prose/semantic inline HTML. Literal source code stays literal.
- Existing external image/media URLs are resolved against the pinned source. Assets are not downloaded or availability-tested; image-only formulas/diagrams still require their source resources.

# <a id="title"></a>CSS Fonts Module Level 4

[Copyright](https://www.w3.org/policies/#copyright) © 2026 [World Wide Web Consortium](https://www.w3.org/). W3C<sup>®</sup> [liability](https://www.w3.org/policies/#Legal_Disclaimer), [trademark](https://www.w3.org/policies/#W3C_Trademarks) and [permissive document license](https://www.w3.org/copyright/software-license/) rules apply.

## <a id="abstract"></a>Abstract

This specification defines modifications to the existing [CSS Fonts 3](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-fonts-3/) specification along with additional features for font feature control, font variations, and chromatic (color) fonts.

[CSS](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS/) is a language for describing the rendering of structured documents (such as HTML and XML) on screen, on paper, etc.

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-language-override"></a>

“At-risk” is a W3C Process term-of-art, and does not necessarily imply that the feature is in danger of being dropped or delayed. It means that the WG believes the feature may have difficulty being interoperably implemented in a timely manner, and marking it as such allows the WG to drop the feature if necessary when transitioning to the Proposed Rec stage, without having to publish a new Candidate Rec without the feature first.

## <a id="introduction"></a>1.  Introduction

This specification includes and expands on the basic font features described in the CSS3 Fonts specification ([\[CSS-FONTS-3\]](#biblio-css-fonts-3)).

### <a id="values"></a>1.1.  Value Definitions

This specification follows the [CSS property definition conventions](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS2/about.html#property-defs) from [\[CSS2\]](#biblio-css2) using the [value definition syntax](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-3/#value-defs) from [\[CSS-VALUES-3\]](#biblio-css-values-3). Value types not defined in this specification are defined in CSS Values &#x26; Units [\[CSS-VALUES-3\]](#biblio-css-values-3). Combination with other CSS modules may expand the definitions of these value types.

<a id="ref-for-css-wide-keywords"></a>

In addition to the property-specific values listed in their definitions, all properties defined in this specification also accept the [CSS-wide keywords](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#css-wide-keywords) as their property value. For readability they have not been repeated explicitly.

### <a id="font-relative"></a>1.2.  Interaction With Font-Relative Units

<a id="ref-for-font-affecting-property"></a>

The following properties defined in this specification are [font-affecting properties](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-values-4/#font-affecting-property):

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font"></a>

  All longhands of the [font](#propdef-font) shorthand

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-synthesis"></a>

  All longhands of the [font-synthesis](#propdef-font-synthesis) shorthand

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant"></a>

  All longhands of the [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) shorthand

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-language-override①"></a>

  [font-language-override](#propdef-font-language-override)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings"></a>

  [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings"></a>

  [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-optical-sizing"></a>

  [font-optical-sizing](#propdef-font-optical-sizing)

## <a id="basic-font-props"></a>2.  Basic Font Properties

The particular font face used to render a character is determined by the font family and other font properties that apply to a given element. This structure allows settings to be varied independently of each other.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-family"></a>

### <a id="font-family-prop"></a>2.1.  Font family: the [font-family](#propdef-font-family) property

| Field               | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
|---------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="propdef-font-family"></a>font-family                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#value-defs">Value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-mult-comma"></a><a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-family"></a><a id="ref-for-comb-one"></a><a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value"></a>\[ [\<font-family-name\>](#font-family-name-value) [\|](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#comb-one) [\<generic-font-family\>](#typedef-generic-font-family) \][\#](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#mult-comma) |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#initial-values">Initial:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | depends on user agent                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#applies-to">Applies to:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | all elements and text                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#inherited-property">Inherited:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | yes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#percentages">Percentages:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | n/a                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#computed">Computed value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-family①"></a>list, each item a string and/or [\<generic-font-family\>](#typedef-generic-font-family) keywords                                                                                                                                                                              |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/cssom/#serializing-css-values">Canonical order:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | per grammar                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/web-animations/#animation-type">Animation type:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | discrete                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

- [standard-font-family-9.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/standard-font-family-9.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/standard-font-family-9.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/standard-font-family-9.html)
- [standard-font-family.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/standard-font-family.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/standard-font-family.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/standard-font-family.html)
- [test_font_family_parsing.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/test_font_family_parsing.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/test_font_family_parsing.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/test_font_family_parsing.html)
- [system-fonts.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/animations/system-fonts.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/animations/system-fonts.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/animations/system-fonts.html)
- [font-family-computed.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-family-computed.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-family-computed.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-family-computed.html)
- [font-family-invalid.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-family-invalid.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-family-invalid.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-family-invalid.html)
- [font-family-valid.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-family-valid.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-family-valid.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-family-valid.html)

This property specifies a prioritized list of font family names or generic family names. A font family defines a set of faces that vary in weight, width or slope. CSS uses the combination of a family name with other font properties to select an individual face. Using this selection mechanism, rather than selecting a face via the style name as is often done in design applications, allows some degree of regularity in textual display when fallback occurs.

Component values are a comma-separated list indicating alternatives. A user agent iterates through the list of family names until it matches an available font that contains a glyph for the character to be rendered. (See [§ 5.3 Cluster matching](#cluster-matching).) This allows for differences in available fonts across platforms and for differences in the range of characters supported by individual fonts.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-font-list"></a>
>
> ```text
> body {
>   font-family: Helvetica, Verdana, sans-serif;
> }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-sans-serif"></a>
>
> If Helvetica is available, it will be used when rendering. If neither Helvetica nor Verdana is present, then the generic font-family [sans-serif](#valdef-font-family-sans-serif) font will be used.

There are three types of font family names, two of which may be used with this property:

<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value①"></a>

<a id="font-family-name-value"></a>[\<font-family-name\>](#font-family-name-value)

The name of a font family, such as Helvetica or Verdana in the previous example.

This might be a locally-instaled font, or might be a web font.

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-family②"></a>

[\<generic-font-family\>](#typedef-generic-font-family)

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-family③"></a>

Each [\<generic-font-family\>](#typedef-generic-font-family) keyword represents a generic font choice, and behaves as a potential alias for one or more locally-installed fonts belonging to the specified generic font category. A <a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-family④"></a>\<generic-font-family\> can thus be used as a fallback for when an author’s more specific font choices are not available.

There are three types of generic family:

1.  <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-monospace"></a>

    Generics which apply to all Unicode characters and will <em>always</em> match a locally installed font. For example, [monospace](#valdef-font-family-monospace).

2.  <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-ui-rounded"></a>

    Generics which apply to all Unicode characters but may not match to a locally installed font on some systems. For example, [ui-rounded](#valdef-font-family-ui-rounded).

3.  <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-generic-fangsong"></a>

    Generics which are writing-system specific, only apply to a subset of Unicode characters, and may not match to a locally installed font on some systems. For example, [generic(fangsong)](#valdef-font-family-generic-fangsong).

Authors are encouraged to append a generic font family as a last alternative for improved robustness, and to use a more specific generic font family, if applicable, to prefer a specific style even if the individual named font family is not available.

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-family⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value②"></a>

Note that [\<generic-font-family\>](#typedef-generic-font-family) keywords cannot be quoted (otherwise they are interpreted as a [\<font-family-name\>](#font-family-name-value)).

The set of generic family keywords is defined in [§ 2.1.5 Generic font families](#generic-font-families).

<a id="ref-for-system-font-family-name-value"></a>

<a id="system-font-family-name-value"></a>[\<system-font-family-name\>](#system-font-family-name-value)

A locally installed system font, whose use is subject to certain constraints. In particular, it may not be used with the font-family property, but can be used with the font shorthand.

The following values refer to system fonts:

<a id="valdef-font-caption"></a>caption  
The font used for captioned controls (e.g., buttons, drop-downs, etc.).

<a id="valdef-font-icon"></a>icon  
The font used to label icons.

<a id="valdef-font-menu"></a>menu  
The font used in menus (e.g., dropdown menus and menu lists).

<a id="valdef-font-message-box"></a>message-box  
The font used in dialog boxes.

<a id="valdef-font-small-caption"></a>small-caption  
The font used for labeling small controls.

<a id="valdef-font-status-bar"></a>status-bar  
The font used in window status bars.

Tests

- [system-fonts-serialization.tentative.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/system-fonts-serialization.tentative.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/system-fonts-serialization.tentative.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/system-fonts-serialization.tentative.html)

<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value③"></a>

#### <a id="font-family-name-syntax"></a>2.1.1.  Syntax of [\<font-family-name\>](#font-family-name-value) 

<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-string-value"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-identifier-value"></a>

<a id="ref-for-mult-one-plus"></a>

```text
<font-family-name> = <string> | <custom-ident>+
```
<a id="ref-for-string-value①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-identifier-value①"></a>

Font family names other than generic families or system font families must either be given quoted as [\<string\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#string-value)s, or unquoted as a sequence of one or more [\<custom-ident\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#identifier-value).

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: This means most punctuation characters and digits at the start of each token must be escaped in unquoted font family names.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-no-unquoted-punctuation"></a> To illustrate this, the following declarations are invalid:
>
> ```text
> font-family: Red/Black, sans-serif;
> font-family: "Lucida" Grande, sans-serif;
> font-family: Ahem!, sans-serif;
> font-family: test@foo, sans-serif;
> font-family: #POUND, sans-serif;
> font-family: Hawaii 5-0, sans-serif;
> ```
<a id="ref-for-css-wide-keywords①"></a>

Any identifier which could be misinterpreted as a pre-defined keyword in the font-family value definition, or the [CSS-wide keywords](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#css-wide-keywords), is not allowed.

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-family⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-css-wide-keywords②"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: this means that if you really have a font whose name is the same as one of the [\<generic-font-family\>](#typedef-generic-font-family) names, or the system font names, or the [CSS-wide keywords](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#css-wide-keywords), it <em>must</em> be quoted.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-valid-unusual-generic-like"></a> To illustrate this, the following unusual font family names are valid because they are quoted:
>
> ```text
> font-family: "sans-serif", sans-serif;
> font-family: "default", sans-serif;
> font-family: "initial", sans-serif;
> font-family: "inherit", sans-serif;
> ```
<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value⑤"></a>

If a sequence of identifiers is given as a [\<font-family-name\>](#font-family-name-value), the computed value is the name converted to a string by joining all the identifiers in the sequence by single spaces.

To avoid mistakes in escaping, it is recommended to quote font family names that contain white space, digits, or punctuation characters other than hyphens:

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-best-quote"></a> Quoting font families prevents escaping mistakes.
>
> ```text
> body { font-family: "New Century Schoolbook", serif }
> 
> <body style="font-family: '21st Century', fantasy">
> ```
<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-family①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-css-wide-keywords③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-all-inherit"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-family⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-serif"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value⑥"></a>

Font family <em>names</em> that happen to be the same as a [font-family](#propdef-font-family) keyword value (e.g. [CSS-wide keywords](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#css-wide-keywords) such as [inherit](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#valdef-all-inherit), or [\<generic-font-family\>](#typedef-generic-font-family) keywords such as [serif](#valdef-font-family-serif)) must be quoted to prevent confusion with the keywords of the same names. UAs must not consider these keywords as matching the [\<font-family-name\>](#font-family-name-value) type.

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-family⑧"></a>

#### <a id="generic-family-name-syntax"></a>2.1.2.  Syntax of [\<generic-font-family\>](#typedef-generic-font-family) 

<a id="typedef-generic-font-family"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-family⑨"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-script-specific"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-complete"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-incomplete"></a>

<a id="typedef-generic-font-script-specific"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-script-specific①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-generic-fangsong①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-generic-kai"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-generic-khmer-mul"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-generic-nastaliq"></a>

<a id="typedef-generic-font-complete"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-complete①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-serif①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-sans-serif①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑧"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-system-ui"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑨"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-cursive"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①⓪"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-fantasy"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-math"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-monospace①"></a>

<a id="typedef-generic-font-incomplete"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-incomplete①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-ui-serif"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-ui-sans-serif"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-ui-monospace"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-ui-rounded①"></a>

```text
<generic-font-family> = <generic-font-script-specific>| <generic-font-complete> | <generic-font-incomplete>
<generic-font-script-specific> = generic(fangsong) | generic(kai) | generic(khmer-mul) |  generic(nastaliq)
<generic-font-complete> = serif | sans-serif | system-ui | cursive | fantasy | math | monospace
<generic-font-incomplete> = ui-serif | ui-sans-serif | ui-monospace | ui-rounded
```
To make the syntax less succeptible to clashes, more recently defined generic font families are identified using a functional syntax.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-generic-functional"></a>
>
> ```text
> body { font-family: "Adobe Fangsong Std R", generic(fangsong), serif}
> ```
>
> The first choice in this example is a specific, named font, in Fang Song (仿宋) style. The family name is enclosed in quotes as it contains space characters. The second is a recently added script-specific generic font; it is unicode-range specific and so may not match to an actual installed font on some systems; but if it exists it will be an example of the requested style. The third is a universal generic font, which is guaranteed to match on all systems.

<a id="ref-for-system-font-family-name-value①"></a>

#### <a id="system"></a>2.1.3.  Syntax of [\<system-font-family-name\>](#system-font-family-name-value) 

<a id="ref-for-system-font-family-name-value②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-caption"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-icon"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-menu"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑧"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-message-box"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑨"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-small-caption"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one②⓪"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-status-bar"></a>

```text
<system-font-family-name> = caption | icon | menu | message-box | small-caption | status-bar
```
#### <a id="font-families"></a>2.1.4.  Relationship Between Faces and Families 

A font family name only specifies a name given to a set of font faces; it does not specify an individual face.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-futura-matches"></a> For example, given the availability of the fonts below, Futura would match but Futura Medium would not:
>
> ![family and face names](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/familyvsfacename.png)
>
> Family and individual face names

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: The CSS definition of font attributes used for selection are explicitly not intended to define a font taxonomy. A type designer’s idea of a family can often extend to a set of faces that vary along axes other than just the standard axes of weight ([font-weight](#propdef-font-weight)), width ([font-width](#propdef-font-width)), and slant ([font-style](#propdef-font-style)). A family can vary along axes that are unique to that family. The CSS font selection mechanism merely provides a way to determine the “closest” match when substitution is necessary.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: The precise way a set of fonts are grouped into font families varies depending upon the platform font management APIs. For example, the Windows GDI API only allows four faces to be grouped into a family, while the DirectWrite API, Core Text API, and other platforms support font families with a variety of weights, widths, and slopes (see [Appendix A: Mapping platform font properties to CSS properties](#platform-props-to-css) for more details).

See [§ 5.1 Localized name matching](#localized-name-matching) below for information on how font-family names are matched.

#### <a id="generic-font-families"></a>2.1.5.  Generic font families

<a id="ref-for-composite-face"></a>

<a id="ref-for-content-language"></a>

A generic font family is a font family which has a standard name (as defined by CSS), but which is an alias for an existing installed font family present on the system. However, a single generic font family may be a [composite face](#composite-face) combining different typefaces based on such things as the Unicode range of the character, the [content language](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-text-4/#content-language) of the containing element, user preferences, system settings, etc. Different generic font families may map to the same used font.

Tests

- [generic-family-keywords-001.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/generic-family-keywords-001.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/generic-family-keywords-001.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/generic-family-keywords-001.html)
- [generic-family-keywords-002.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/generic-family-keywords-002.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/generic-family-keywords-002.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/generic-family-keywords-002.html)

<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value⑦"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: Generic font families are intended to be widely implemented on many platforms, unlike arbitrary [\<font-family-name\>](#font-family-name-value)s which are usually platform-specific names. They are expected to map to different fonts on different platforms. Authors may specify these generic family names if they desire their text to follow a particular design on many platforms, and are not particular about which specific font is chosen on those platforms.

User agents should provide reasonable default choices for the generic font families, that express the characteristics of each family as well as possible, within the limits allowed by the underlying technology. User agents are encouraged to allow users to select alternative faces for the generic font families.

<a id="serif-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-serif"></a>serif  
<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-sans-serif②"></a>

Serif fonts represent glyphs that have finishing strokes, flared or tapering ends, or have actual serifed endings (including slab serifs). Serif fonts are typically proportionately-spaced. They often display a greater variation between thick and thin strokes than fonts from the [sans-serif](#valdef-font-family-sans-serif) generic font family.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-serif②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-sans-serif③"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: [serif](#valdef-font-family-serif) and [sans-serif](#valdef-font-family-sans-serif) only apply to a small handful of writing scripts. Their use as generic font families in CSS is historical, and reflects the Latin-centric nature of early Web development. Better and more widely applicable names would have been, for example, "modulated" and "monoline". However, for reasons of Web compatibility, these names cannot be changed.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-serif③"></a>

CSS uses the term "serif" to apply to a font for any script, although other names might be more familiar for particular scripts, such as Mincho (Japanese), Sung or Song (Chinese), Batang (Korean). For Arabic, the Naskh style would correspond to [serif](#valdef-font-family-serif).

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-serif④"></a>

[serif](#valdef-font-family-serif) must always map to at least one matched font face.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-serif⑤"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: No guarantee is placed on the character coverage of that font face. Therefore, the font [serif](#valdef-font-family-serif) is mapped to may not end up being used for all content.

![sample serif fonts](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/serifexamples.png)

Sample serif fonts

<a id="sans-serif-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-sans-serif"></a>sans-serif  
<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-serif⑥"></a>

Glyphs in sans-serif fonts, as the term is used in CSS, are generally low contrast (vertical and horizontal stems have the close to the same thickness) and have stroke endings that are plain (without any flaring, cross stroke, or other ornamentation). Sans-serif fonts are typically proportionately-spaced. They often have little variation between thick and thin strokes, compared to fonts from the [serif](#valdef-font-family-serif) family. CSS uses the term "sans-serif" to apply to a font for any script, although other names might be more familiar for particular scripts, such as Gothic (Japanese), Hei (Chinese), or Gulim (Korean).

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-sans-serif④"></a>

[sans-serif](#valdef-font-family-sans-serif) must always map to at least one matched font face.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-sans-serif⑤"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: No guarantee is placed on the character coverage of that font face. Therefore, the font [sans-serif](#valdef-font-family-sans-serif) is mapped to may not end up being used for all content.

![sample sans-serif fonts](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/sansserifexamples.png)

Sample sans-serif fonts

<a id="cursive-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-cursive"></a>cursive  
Glyphs in cursive fonts generally use a script style, and the result looks more like handwritten pen or brush writing than printed letterwork. CSS uses the term "cursive" to apply to a font for any script, although other names such as Chancery, Brush, Swing and Script are also used in font names.

![sample cursive fonts](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/cursiveexamples.png)

Sample cursive fonts

<a id="fantasy-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-fantasy"></a>fantasy  
Fantasy fonts are primarily decorative or expressive fonts that contain decorative or expressive representations of characters. These do not include Pi or Picture fonts which do not represent actual characters.

![sample fantasy fonts](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/fantasyexamples.png)

Sample fantasy fonts

<a id="monospace-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-monospace"></a>monospace  
The sole criterion of a monospace font is that all glyphs have the same fixed width. This is often used to render samples of computer code.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-monospace②"></a>

[monospace](#valdef-font-family-monospace) must always map to at least one matched font face.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-monospace③"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: No guarantee is placed on the character coverage of that font face. Therefore, the font [monospace](#valdef-font-family-monospace) is mapped to may not end up being used for all content.

![sample monospace fonts](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/monospaceexamples.png)

Sample monospace fonts

<a id="system-ui-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-system-ui"></a>system-ui  
<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-system-ui①"></a>

This generic font family lets text render with the default user interface font (which, as with all [§ 2.1.5 Generic font families](#generic-font-families), may be a composite font) on the platform on which the UA is running. A cross-platform UA should use different fonts on its different supported platforms. The purpose of [system-ui](#valdef-font-family-system-ui) is to allow web content to integrate with the look and feel of the native OS. This is useful for hybrid, web or cross-platform applications.

Tests

- [system-ui-ar.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/system-ui-ar.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/system-ui-ar.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/system-ui-ar.html)
- [system-ui-ja-vs-zh.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/system-ui-ja-vs-zh.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/system-ui-ja-vs-zh.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/system-ui-ja-vs-zh.html)

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-system-ui②"></a>

The precise font(s) used will depend on factors such as platform support, platform language, locale settings, user preferences, Unicode coverage and the content language. UA may optionally choose to use the text rendering engine provided by the platform when using [system-ui](#valdef-font-family-system-ui) to match the appearance of the default user interface more closely.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-system-ui③"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-system-ui-opaque"></a> As with other generic font families, the substitution of specific installed fonts for [system-ui](#valdef-font-family-system-ui) does not affect the computed style.
> ```text
> <div id="system-text" style="font-family: system-ui"></div>
> ...
> window.getComputedStyle(document.getElementById("system-text")).getPropertyValue("font-family");
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-system-ui④"></a>
>
> The script above should not have any knowledge of how [system-ui](#valdef-font-family-system-ui) is expanded to include a collection of system user interface fonts. In particular, the above script should yield a result of "system-ui" on every platform.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-system-ui⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-sans-serif⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-serif⑦"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: [system-ui](#valdef-font-family-system-ui) is highly dependent on the platform environment as multiple factors may affect the default user interface font, and may have verying level of text rendering support. This may cause the displayed typeface to be undesirable for some end users (e.g. Simplified Chinese users might get a monospaced serif font on older version of Windows; lang attribute might not affect displayed font in cases like lang="uk" in Russian locale or lang="ja" in Chinese locale). Some operating system vendors does not offer means to customise <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-system-ui⑥"></a>system-ui, unlike UAs that generally allow users to customise the generic [sans-serif](#valdef-font-family-sans-serif) or [serif](#valdef-font-family-serif) font family. As the name implies, <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-system-ui⑦"></a>system-ui is intended for use with UI elements in web applications, and may or may not be suitable for large paragraphs of text or articles, depending on operating system vendor implementation.

<a id="math-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-math"></a>math  
This font family is intended for use with mathematical expressions.

Such fonts may include additional data (for example, the OpenType MATH table) to help with the hierarchical process of equation layout. In particular, they may contain stylistic and stretching glyph variants useful in setting mathematical equations.

<a id="generic(fangsong)-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-generic-fangsong"></a>generic(fangsong)  
<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-generic-kai①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-serif⑧"></a>

This font family is used for Fang Song (仿宋) typefaces in Chinese. Fang Song is a relaxed, intermediate form between Song ([serif](#valdef-font-family-serif)) and Kai ([generic(kai)](#valdef-font-family-generic-kai)). Typically, the horizontal lines are tilted, the endpoint flourishes are smaller, and there is less variation in stroke width, compared to a Song style. Fang Song is often used for official Chinese Government documents.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-generic-fangsong②"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: [generic(fangsong)](#valdef-font-family-generic-fangsong) might not map to any locally installed font, but if it does, that font will be in Fang Song style.

![](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/fangsong.svg)

Chinese text displayed with sample fang song fonts

<a id="generic(kai)-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-generic-kai"></a>generic(kai)  
This font family is used in Simplified &#x26; Traditional Chinese. A major typeface, which provides calligraphic styles for Chinese text. It shows notable handwriting features. Kai is commonly used in official documents and textbooks. Most official documents in Taiwan use Kai in full text. Kai can also be combined with other typefaces to be used in text that needs to be differentiated from the rest of the content, for example, headlines, references, quotations, and dialogs.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-generic-kai②"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: [generic(kai)](#valdef-font-family-generic-kai) might not map to any locally installed font, but if it does, that font will be in Kai style.

![](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/kaiti.svg)

Chinese text displayed with sample kai fonts

<a id="generic(khmer-mul)-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-generic-khmer-mul"></a>generic(khmer-mul)  
This font family is used in the Khmer language (used in Cambodia), for titles and headings, or to emphasise important names or nouns. Compared to the upright (âksâr chôr) style, which is used for regular text, âksâr mul is characterized by heavier, more rounded letter shapes, and may include more ligated forms.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-generic-khmer-mul①"></a>

Despite the visually heavier style, the font weight for regular text in [generic(khmer-mul)](#valdef-font-family-generic-khmer-mul) is unchanged (400).

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-generic-khmer-mul②"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: [generic(khmer-mul)](#valdef-font-family-generic-khmer-mul) might not map to any locally installed font, but if it does, that font will be in âksâr mul style.

![](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/vi-tu-comparison.png)

Cambodian text displayed in: (top) âksâr chôr style, set in Khmer OS Battambang; (center) âksâr mul style, set in Khmer OS Moul v6, with no ligatures; (bottom) âksâr mul style, set in Khmer OS Moul v4, with two ligatures. All fonts by Dan Hong.

<a id="generic(nastaliq)-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-generic-nastaliq"></a>generic(nastaliq)  
This font family is the standard way of writing Urdu and Kashmiri, and is also often a preferred style for Persian and other language text, especially in literary genres such as poetry. Key features include a sloping baseline for joined letters, and overall complex shaping and positioning for base letters and diacritics alike. There are also distinctive shapes for many glyphs and ligatures. It is important not to fall back to a naskh style for languages such as Urdu and Kashmiri.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-generic-nastaliq①"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: [generic(nastaliq)](#valdef-font-family-generic-nastaliq) might not map to any locally installed font, but if it does, that font will be in Nastaliq style.

![السلام علیکم](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/urdu-nastaliq.svg)

Urdu text displayed with a sample nastaliq font (Noto Nastaliq Urdu)

![السلام علیکم](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/urdu-naskh.svg)

Urdu text displayed with a fallback naskh font (Scheherazade New)

<a id="ui-serif-def"></a><a id="valdef-font-family-ui-serif"></a>ui-serif  
<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-ui-serif①"></a>

This font family is used for the serif variant of the system’s user interface. The purpose of [ui-serif](#valdef-font-family-ui-serif) is to allow web content to integrate with the look and feel of the native OS.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-ui-serif②"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: [ui-serif](#valdef-font-family-ui-serif) is not expected to map to any font on platforms without an appropriate system font.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-system-ui⑧"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight②"></a>

The [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight) property specifies the weight of glyphs in the font, their degree of blackness, or stroke thickness.

This property accepts values of the following:

<a id="font-weight-absolute-values"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one②③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one②④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-number-value"></a>

```text
<font-weight-absolute> = [ normal | bold | <number [1,1000]> ]
```
Values have the following meanings:

<a id="ref-for-number-value①"></a>

<a id="font-weight-numeric-values"></a><a id="valdef-font-weight-number-1-1000"></a>[\<number \[1,1000\]\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#number-value)

<a id="ref-for-css-invalid"></a>

Each number indicates a weight that is at least as dark as its predecessor. Only values greater than or equal to 1, and less than or equal to 1000, are valid, and all other values are [invalid](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-syntax-3/#css-invalid). Numeric values typically correspond to the commonly used weight names below.

- 100 - Thin
- 200 - Extra Light (Ultra Light)
- 300 - Light
- 400 - Normal
- 500 - Medium
- 600 - Semi Bold (Demi Bold)
- 700 - Bold
- 800 - Extra Bold (Ultra Bold)
- 900 - Black (Heavy)

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: A font might internally provide its own weight name mappings, but those mappings within the font are disregarded in CSS.

<a id="valdef-font-weight-normal"></a>normal

Same as 400.

<a id="valdef-font-weight-bold"></a>bold

Same as 700.

<a id="valdef-font-weight-bolder"></a>bolder

Specifies a bolder weight than the inherited value. See [§ 2.2.1 Relative Weights](#relative-weights).

<a id="valdef-font-weight-lighter"></a>lighter

Specifies a lighter weight than the inherited value. See [§ 2.2.1 Relative Weights](#relative-weights).

Font formats that use a scale other than a nine-step scale should map their scale onto the CSS scale so that 400 roughly corresponds with a face that would be labeled as Regular, Book, Roman and 700 roughly matches a face that would be labeled as Bold. Alternately, weights may be inferred from style names that correspond roughly with the scale above. The scale is relative, so a face with a larger weight value must never appear lighter. If style names are used to infer weights, care should be taken to handle variations in style names across locales.

#### <a id="relative-weights"></a>2.2.1.  Relative Weights

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-weight-bolder"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-weight-lighter"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight③"></a>

Specified values of [bolder](#valdef-font-weight-bolder) and [lighter](#valdef-font-weight-lighter) indicate weights relative to the weight of the parent element. The computed weight is calculated based on the inherited [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight) value using the chart below.

<a id="bolderlighter"></a>

| Inherited value (<var>w</var>) | bolder    | lighter   |
|---------------------------------------|-----------|-----------|
| <var>w</var> \< 100            | 400       | No change |
| 100 ≤ <var>w</var> \< 350      | 400       | 100       |
| 350 ≤ <var>w</var> \< 550      | 700       | 100       |
| 550 ≤ <var>w</var> \< 750      | 900       | 400       |
| 750 ≤ <var>w</var> \< 900      | 900       | 700       |
| 900 ≤ <var>w</var>             | No change | 700       |

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: The table above is equivalent to selecting the next relative bolder or lighter face, given a font family containing normal and bold faces along with a thin and a heavy face. Authors who desire finer control over the exact weight values used for a given element can use numerical values instead of relative weights.

#### <a id="missing-weights"></a>2.2.2.  Missing weights

Quite often there are only a few weights available for a particular font family. When a weight is specified for which no face exists, a face with a nearby weight is used. In general, bold weights map to faces with heavier weights and light weights map to faces with lighter weights. (See the [§ 5 Font Matching Algorithm](#font-matching-algorithm) for a precise definition.)

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-nearby-weights"></a> The examples here illustrate which face is used for different weights. Grey indicates that a face for the desired weight does not exist, so a face with a nearby weight is used.
>
> ![weight mappings for a family with 400, 700 and 900 weights](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/optimaweights.png)
>
> Weight mappings for a font family with 400, 700 and 900 weight faces
>
> ![weight mappings for a family with 300, 600 weights](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/hiraginoweights.png)
>
> Weight mappings for a font family with 300 and 600 weight faces

Most user agents model a font as having a particular weight which often corresponds to one of the numbers in the nine-step scale described [above](#font-weight-numeric-values). While this is true of most fonts, some fonts might be configurable so as to support a range of weights. In this situation, the user agent uses a face with a weight as close as possible to the weight requested (see [§ 5 Font Matching Algorithm](#font-matching-algorithm) for the precise algorithm). In particular, a user agent using a font which supports a range of weights should behave the same as if a font is present at each individual weight in the range. For TrueType / OpenType fonts that use variations, the `wght` variation is used to implement varying weights. Fractional weights are valid.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-synthesis①"></a>

Although the practice is not well-loved by typographers, bold faces are often synthesized by user agents for families that lack actual bold faces. For the purposes of font matching, these faces must be treated as if they exist within the family. Authors can explicitly avoid this behavior by using the [font-synthesis](#propdef-font-synthesis) property.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width①"></a>

### <a id="font-width-prop"></a>2.3.  Font width: the [font-width](#propdef-font-width) property

| Field               | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|---------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="propdef-font-width"></a>font-width                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#value-defs">Value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-percentage-value"></a><a id="ref-for-comb-one②⑤"></a>normal [\|](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#comb-one) [\<percentage \[0,∞\]\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#percentage-value) <a id="ref-for-comb-one②⑥"></a>\| ultra-condensed <a id="ref-for-comb-one②⑦"></a>\| extra-condensed <a id="ref-for-comb-one②⑧"></a>\| condensed <a id="ref-for-comb-one②⑨"></a>\| semi-condensed <a id="ref-for-comb-one③⓪"></a>\| semi-expanded <a id="ref-for-comb-one③①"></a>\| expanded <a id="ref-for-comb-one③②"></a>\| extra-expanded <a id="ref-for-comb-one③③"></a>\| ultra-expanded |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#initial-values">Initial:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | normal                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#applies-to">Applies to:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | all elements and text                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#inherited-property">Inherited:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | yes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#percentages">Percentages:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | Not resolved                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#computed">Computed value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | a percentage, see below                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/cssom/#serializing-css-values">Canonical order:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | per grammar                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/web-animations/#animation-type">Animation type:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | by computed value type                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width②"></a>

The [font-width](#propdef-font-width) property selects a normal, condensed, or expanded face from a font family. Values are specified either as percentages or as keywords which map to a percentage as defined in the following table:

<a id="widthmappings"></a>

| Absolute keyword value             | Numeric value |
|------------------------------------|---------------|
| <a id="valdef-font-width-ultra-condensed"></a>ultra-condensed | 50%           |
| <a id="valdef-font-width-extra-condensed"></a>extra-condensed | 62.5%         |
| <a id="valdef-font-width-condensed"></a>condensed       | 75%           |
| <a id="valdef-font-width-semi-condensed"></a>semi-condensed  | 87.5%         |
| <a id="valdef-font-width-normal"></a>normal          | 100%          |
| <a id="valdef-font-width-semi-expanded"></a>semi-expanded   | 112.5%        |
| <a id="valdef-font-width-expanded"></a>expanded        | 125%          |
| <a id="valdef-font-width-extra-expanded"></a>extra-expanded  | 150%          |
| <a id="valdef-font-width-ultra-expanded"></a>ultra-expanded  | 200%          |

<a id="ref-for-percentage-value①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-css-invalid①"></a>

<a id="valdef-font-width-percentage-0"></a>[\<percentage \[0,∞\]\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#percentage-value) values represent the fractional width of the glyphs, with 100% representing “normal” glyph widths (as defined by the font designer). Values less than 0% are [invalid](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-syntax-3/#css-invalid).

When a face does not exist for a given width, values less than 100% map to a narrower face if one exists, otherwise a wider face. Conversely, values greater than or equal to 100% map to a wider face if one exists, otherwise a narrower face. Some fonts might support a range of width values; if the requested width value is not available in the font, the closest supported value is used, using the same mapping rules (see the [§ 5 Font Matching Algorithm](#font-matching-algorithm) for the precise algorithm). For TrueType / OpenType fonts that support variations, the `wdth` variation is used to implement varying widths.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-font-width-matching"></a> The figure below shows how nine font-width property settings affect font matching for a font family containing a variety of discrete widths. Grey indicates a width for which no face exists and a different width is substituted:
>
> ![width mappings for a family with condensed, normal and expanded faces](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/universwidths.png)
>
> Width mappings for a font family with condensed, normal and expanded width faces

<a id="ref-for-dom-window-getcomputedstyle"></a>

<a id="ref-for-percentage-value②"></a>

<code><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/cssom-1/#dom-window-getcomputedstyle">getComputedStyle()</a></code> always serializes its value as a [\<percentage\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#percentage-value), regardless of how the value was specified by the author, or whether or not a keyword happens to map to the value.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-stretch"></a>

#### <a id="font-stretch-prop"></a>2.3.1.  Font width: the [font-stretch](#propdef-font-stretch) legacy name alias

<a id="ref-for-legacy-name-alias"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width③"></a>

For historical reasons, a <a id="propdef-font-stretch"></a>font-stretch property exists which is a [legacy name alias](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#legacy-name-alias) and functions in the identical way to the [font-width](#propdef-font-width).

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-stretch①"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-font-stretch-set"></a> For example, here the legacy [font-stretch](#propdef-font-stretch) is used on level one headings.
>
> ```text
> h1 {font-stretch: condensed; }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width④"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-width-condensed"></a>
>
> The specified value of the [font-width](#propdef-font-width) on those headings becomes set to [condensed](#valdef-font-width-condensed)'.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width⑤"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-font-width-set"></a> For example, here the [font-width](#propdef-font-width) is used on level one headings.
>
> ```text
> h1 {font-width: condensed; }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-stretch②"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-width-condensed①"></a>
>
> The specified value of the [font-stretch](#propdef-font-stretch) on those headings becomes set to [condensed](#valdef-font-width-condensed).

User agents must not synthesize condensed or expanded faces for font families which lack such faces and which do not have a width variation axis. In particular, user agents must not geometrically stretch such faces.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style②"></a>

The [font-style](#propdef-font-style) property allows italic or oblique faces to be selected. Italic forms are generally cursive in nature while oblique faces are typically sloped versions of the regular face.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-faux-vs-italic"></a> Compare the artificially sloped renderings of Palatino "a" and Baskerville "N" in grey with the actual italic versions:
>
> ![artificial sloping vs. real italics](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/realvsfakeitalics.png)
>
> Artificial sloping versus real italics

Values have the following meanings:

<a id="valdef-font-style-normal"></a>normal

Matches against a face that is classified as a normal face, one that is neither italic or obliqued. This represents an oblique value of "0".

<a id="valdef-font-style-italic"></a>italic

Matches against a font that is labeled as an italic face, or an oblique face if one does not exist. The angle and direction of slant is unspecified.

<a id="valdef-font-style-left"></a>left

Matches against a font that is labeled as an italic face, with a positive (clockwise) slant; or an oblique face with positive slant, if one does not exist.

<a id="valdef-font-style-right"></a>right

Matches against a font that is labeled as an italic face, with a negative (counter-clockwise) slant; or an oblique face with negative slant, if one does not exist.

<a id="ref-for-angle-value①"></a>

<a id="valdef-font-style-oblique-angle--90deg-90deg"></a>oblique [\<angle \[-90deg,90deg\]\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#angle-value)?

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-synthesis-style"></a>

<a id="ref-for-css-invalid②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-angle-value②"></a>

Controls matching against an oblique face. Positive angles represent a clockwise slant; negative angles represent a counter-clockwise slant. The lack of an [\<angle\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#angle-value) represents 14deg. (Note that a font might internally provide its own mapping for "oblique", but the mapping within the font is disregarded.) Fractional and negative values are accepted; however, values less than -90deg or values greater than 90deg are [invalid](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-syntax-3/#css-invalid). If no oblique faces exist, and [font-synthesis-style](#propdef-font-synthesis-style) has the value auto, a synthetic oblique face will be generated.

![negative slant](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/contra-italic.png)

An italic face with negative slant. The font is Mf Autumn Breeze, by Misti Hammers.

A font family might contain no italic or oblique faces, only an italic face and no oblique, only an oblique face and no italic, both an oblique and an italic, multiple oblique faces at various angles, or various combinations thereof. The font matching routine will select a font to use which is of the correct sign as the requested angle, and (if there are multiple such faces) closest to the requested angle. In general, for a requested angle greater or equal to 11deg, larger angles are preferred; otherwise, smaller angles are preferred. (See [§ 5 Font Matching Algorithm](#font-matching-algorithm).) For TrueType / OpenType fonts that use variations, the `slnt` variation is used to implement oblique values, and the `ital` variation with a value of 1 is used to implement the italic values.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: the OpenType `slnt` axis is defined with a positive angle meaning a counter-clockwise slant, the opposite direction to CSS. The CSS implementation will take this into account when using variations to produce oblique faces.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="issue">Issue</strong>
>
> <a id="issue-d46def88"></a> What direction should positive and negative obliques skew in vertical writing mode? How do we achieve skews in the opposite dimension (needed for vertical writing)?

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-synthesis②"></a>

If no italic or oblique face is available, oblique faces may be synthesized by rendering non-obliqued faces with an artificial obliquing operation. The use of these artificially obliqued faces can be disabled using the [font-synthesis](#propdef-font-synthesis) property.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: While oblique faces can be simulated by artificially sloping the glyphs of the regular face, this is not equivalent to a true oblique, in which optical stroke thicknesses are properly preserved despite the slant. It is always better to use an actual oblique font rather than rely on a synthetic version.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-style-italic"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-style-oblique"></a>

For the purposes of font matching, User agents may treat [italic](#valdef-font-style-italic) as a synonym for [oblique](https://drafts.csswg.org/css2/#valdef-font-style-oblique). For user agents that treat these values distinctly, synthesis must not be performed for <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-style-italic①"></a>italic.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: Authors should also be aware that synthesized approaches might not be suitable for scripts like Cyrillic, where italic forms are very different in shape. It is always better to use an actual italic font rather than rely on a synthetic version.

<a id="ref-for-character-map"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: Many scripts lack the tradition of mixing a cursive form within text rendered with a normal face. Chinese, Japanese and Korean fonts almost always lack italic or oblique faces. Fonts that support a mixture of scripts will sometimes omit specific scripts, such as Arabic, from the set of glyphs supported in the italic face. User agents should be careful about making [character map](#character-map) assumptions across faces when implementing synthesis across fonts, as italic faces in a family can have different <a id="ref-for-character-map①"></a>character maps than Roman faces.

| Field               | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

- [font-size-valid.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-size-valid.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-size-valid.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-size-valid.html)

This property indicates the desired height of glyphs from the font. For scalable fonts, the font-size is a scale factor applied to the EM unit of the font. For non-scalable fonts, the font-size is converted into absolute units and matched against the declared font-size of the font, using the same absolute coordinate space for both of the matched values.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-containing-block"></a>

<a id="ref-for-ink-overflow"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: There is no requirement on how closely a glyph should fit its EM box. Individual fonts can have a different apparent visual size when rendered at the same [font-size](#propdef-font-size). Additionally, glyphs might render arbitrarily far outside their EM box; and if they overflow their [containing block](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-display-4/#containing-block) can induce [ink overflow](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-overflow-3/#ink-overflow).

Values have the following meanings:

<a id="ref-for-typedef-absolute-size①"></a>

<a id="typedef-absolute-size"></a>[\<absolute-size\>](#typedef-absolute-size)

<a id="ref-for-typedef-absolute-size②"></a>

An [\<absolute-size\>](#typedef-absolute-size) keyword refers to an entry in a table of font sizes computed and kept by the user agent. See [§ 2.5.1 Absolute Size Keyword Mapping Table](#absolute-size-mapping).

Possible values are:

<a id="ref-for-comb-one④①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one④②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one④③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one④④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one④⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one④⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one④⑦"></a>

```text
[ xx-small | x-small | small | medium | large | x-large | xx-large | xxx-large ]
```
<a id="ref-for-typedef-relative-size①"></a>

<a id="typedef-relative-size"></a>[\<relative-size\>](#typedef-relative-size)

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-relative-size②"></a>

A [\<relative-size\>](#typedef-relative-size) keyword is interpreted relative to the computed [font-size](#propdef-font-size) of the parent element and possibly the table of font sizes. Possible values are:

<a id="ref-for-comb-one④⑧"></a>

```text
[ larger | smaller ]
```
If the parent element has a keyword font size in the absolute size keyword mapping table, larger may compute the font size to the next entry in the table, and smaller may compute the font size to the previous entry in the table. For example, if the parent element has a font size of font-size:medium, specifying a value of larger may make the font size of the child element font-size:large.

Instead of using next and previous items in the previous keyword table, User agents may instead use a simple ratio to increase or decrease the font size relative to the parent element. The specific ratio is unspecified, but should be around 1.2–1.5. This ratio may vary across different elements.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: A sight-impaired user may request a user agent use a higher ratio than default, in order to aid readability. In addition, a user agent may choose to use different ratios when it detects paragraph text as opposed to title text.

<a id="ref-for-typedef-length-percentage①"></a>

<a id="valdef-font-size-length-percentage-0"></a>[\<length-percentage \[0,∞\]\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#typedef-length-percentage)

A length value specifies an absolute font size (independent of the user agent’s font table). Negative lengths are invalid.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size③"></a>

A percentage value specifies an absolute font size relative to the parent element’s computed [font-size](#propdef-font-size). Negative percentages are invalid.

<a id="ref-for-font-relative-length"></a>

<a id="ref-for-em"></a>

<a id="ref-for-rem"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: Use of percentage values or [font-relative lengths](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#font-relative-length) such as [em](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#em)s and [rem](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#rem)s leads to more robust and cascadable style sheets.

<a id="font-size-math"></a><a id="valdef-font-size-math"></a>math

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size④"></a>

Special [mathematical scaling rules](https://w3c.github.io/mathml-core/#the-math-script-level-property) must be applied when determining the computed value of the [font-size](#propdef-font-size) property.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-specify-size"></a> The following style sheet demonstrates various ways of specifying the font size.
>
> ```text
> p { font-size: 12pt; }
> blockquote { font-size: larger }
> em { font-size: 150% }
> em { font-size: 1.5em }
> ```
<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size⑤"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: The used value of this property can differ from its computed value due to [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust). However, child elements inherit the computed [font-size](#propdef-font-size) value, which is not affected by <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust①"></a>font-size-adjust (otherwise, the effect of <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust②"></a>font-size-adjust would compound).

The actual value of this property can differ from the used value due to the unavailability of certain font sizes.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-clamp-size"></a> font-size can be clamped, for responsive typography.
>
> ```text
> font-size: clamp(10px, ..., 36px);
> ```
#### <a id="absolute-size-mapping"></a>2.5.1.  Absolute Size Keyword Mapping Table

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-size-medium"></a>

The following table provides user agent guidelines for the absolute-size scaling factor and their mapping to HTML heading and absolute font-sizes. The [medium](https://drafts.csswg.org/css2/#valdef-font-size-medium) value is used as the reference middle value. The user agent may fine-tune these values for different fonts or different types of display devices.

| CSS absolute-size values | xx-small | x-small | small | medium | large | x-large | xx-large | xxx-large |
|--------------------------|----------|---------|-------|--------|-------|---------|----------|-----------|
| <strong>scaling factor&#xA;&#x9;&#x9;&#x9;&#x9;&#xA;      </strong>      | 3/5      | 3/4     | 8/9   | 1      | 6/5   | 3/2     | 2/1      | 3/1       |
| <strong>HTML headings&#xA;&#x9;&#x9;&#x9;&#x9;&#xA;      </strong>      | h6       |         | h5    | h4     | h3    | h2      | h1       |           |
| <strong><span><a id="ref-for-font"></a></span>HTML <span><code><a href="https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/obsolete.html#font">font</a></code></span> sizes&#xA;&#x9;&#x9;&#x9;&#x9;&#xA;      </strong>      | 1        |         | 2     | 3      | 4     | 5       | 6        | 7         |

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: In CSS1, the suggested scaling factor between adjacent indexes was 1.5, which user experience proved to be too large. In CSS2, the suggested scaling factor for computer screen between adjacent indexes was 1.2 which still created issues for the small sizes. The new scaling factor varies between each index to provide a better readability.

To preserve readability, an UA applying these guidelines should nevertheless avoid creating font sizes of less than 9 device pixels per EM unit.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust③"></a>

### <a id="font-size-adjust-prop"></a>2.6.  Relative sizing: the [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust) property

| Field               | Definition                                                                                                                                                           |
|---------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="propdef-font-size-adjust"></a>font-size-adjust                                                                                                                                  |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#value-defs">Value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-number-value②"></a><a id="ref-for-comb-one④⑨"></a>none [\|](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#comb-one) [\<number \[0,∞\]\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#number-value) |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#initial-values">Initial:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | none                                                                                                                                                                 |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#applies-to">Applies to:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | all elements and text                                                                                                                                                |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#inherited-property">Inherited:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | yes                                                                                                                                                                  |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#percentages">Percentages:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | N/A                                                                                                                                                                  |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#computed">Computed value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-font-size-adjust-none-value"></a>a number or the keyword [none](#font-size-adjust-none-value)                                                                                      |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/cssom/#serializing-css-values">Canonical order:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | per grammar                                                                                                                                                          |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/web-animations/#animation-type">Animation type:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | by computed value type                                                                                                                                               |
