For any given font size, the apparent size and effective legibility of text varies across fonts. For bicameral scripts such as Latin or Cyrillic that distinguish between upper and lowercase letters, the relative height of lowercase letters compared to their uppercase counterparts is a determining factor of legibility. This is commonly referred to as the <a id="aspect-value"></a>aspect value and is equal to the x-height of a font divided by the font size.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: For text which uses diacritics, too large an x-height will actually decrease legibility as the diacritics become cramped.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust④"></a>

In situations where font fallback occurs, fallback fonts might not share the same aspect value as the desired font family and will thus be less readable. The [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust) property is a way to preserve the readability of text when font fallback occurs. It does this by adjusting the font-size so that the x-height is the same regardless of the font used.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-verdana-futura"></a> The style defined below defines Verdana as the desired font family, but if Verdana is not available Futura or Times will be used. One paragraph also has font-size-adjust specified.
>
> ```text
> p {
>   font-family: Verdana, Futura, Times;
> }
> p.adj {
>   font-size-adjust: 0.545;
> }
> 
> <p>Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet, ...</p>
> <p class="adj">Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet, ...</p>
> ```
>
> Verdana has a relatively high aspect value of 0.545, meaning lowercase letters are relatively tall compared to uppercase letters, so at small sizes text appears legible. Times has a lower aspect value of 0.447, and so if fallback occurs, the text will be less legible at small sizes than Verdana unless font-size-adjust is also specified.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust⑤"></a>

How text rendered in each of these fonts compares is shown below, the columns show text rendered in Verdana, Futura and Times. The same font-size value is used across cells within each row and red lines are included to show the differences in x-height. In the upper half, each row is rendered in the same font-size value. The same is true for the lower half, but in this half the [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust) property is also set to 0.545, so that the actual font size is adjusted to preserve the x-height of Verdana across each row. Note how small text remains relatively legible across each row in the lower half.

![text with and without 'font-size-adjust'](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/fontsizeadjust.png)

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust⑥"></a>

Text with and without the use of [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust)

<a id="ref-for-aspect-value"></a>

This property allows authors to specify an [aspect value](#aspect-value) for an element that will effectively preserve the x-height of the first choice font, whether it is substituted or not. Values have the following meanings:

<a id="font-size-adjust-none-value"></a>none

Do not preserve the font’s x-height.

<a id="ref-for-number-value③"></a>

<a id="aspect-ratio-value"></a>[\<number \[0,∞\]\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#number-value)

<a id="ref-for-aspect-value①"></a>

Specifies the [aspect value](#aspect-value) used in the calculation below to calculate the adjusted font size:

```text
c  =  ( a / a' ) s
```
where:

<a id="ref-for-aspect-value②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-aspect-value③"></a>

```text
s  =  font-size value
a  =  aspect value as specified by the 'font-size-adjust' property
a' =  aspect value of actual font
c  =  adjusted font-size to use
```
Negative values are invalid.

<a id="ref-for-aspect-value④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust⑦"></a>

This value applies to any font that is selected but in typical usage it should be based on the [aspect value](#aspect-value) of the first font in the font-family list. If this is specified accurately, the `(a/a')` term in the formula above is effectively 1 for the first font and no adjustment occurs. If the value is specified inaccurately, text rendered using the first font in the family list will display differently in older user agents that don’t support [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust).

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust⑧"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-line-height"></a>

The value of [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust) affects the used value of [font-size](#propdef-font-size) but does not affect the computed value. It affects the size of relative units that are based on font metrics such as `ex` and `ch` but does not affect the size of `em` units. Since numeric values of [line-height](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS2/visudet.html#propdef-line-height) refer to the computed size of <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size⑦"></a>font-size, <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust⑨"></a>font-size-adjust does not affect the used value of <a id="ref-for-propdef-line-height①"></a>line-height.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-line-height②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size⑧"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust①⓪"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: In CSS, authors often specify [line-height](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS2/visudet.html#propdef-line-height) as a multiple of the [font-size](#propdef-font-size). Since the [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust) property affects the used value of <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size⑨"></a>font-size, authors should take care setting the line height when <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust①①"></a>font-size-adjust is used. Setting the line height too tightly can result in overlapping lines of text in this situation.

<a id="ref-for-aspect-value⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust①②"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-calc-aspect"></a> Authors can calculate the [aspect value](#aspect-value) for a given font by comparing spans with the same content but different [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust) properties. If the same font-size is used, the spans will match when the <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust①③"></a>font-size-adjust value is accurate for the given font.
>
> <a id="ref-for-aspect-value⑥"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size①⓪"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust①④"></a>
>
> Two spans with borders are used to determine the [aspect value](#aspect-value) of a font. The [font-size](#propdef-font-size) is the same for both spans but the [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust) property is specified only for the right span. Starting with a value of 0.5, the aspect value can be adjusted until the borders around the two letters line up.
>
> ```text
> p {
>   font-family: Futura;
>   font-size: 500px;
> }
> 
> span {
>   border: solid 1px red;
> }
> 
> .adjust {
>   font-size-adjust: 0.5;
> }
> 
> <p><span>b</span><span class="adjust">b</span></p>
> ```
>
> ![Futura with an aspect value of 0.5](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/beforefontsizeadjust.png)
>
> <a id="ref-for-aspect-value⑦"></a>
>
> Futura with an [aspect value](#aspect-value) of 0.5
>
> <a id="ref-for-aspect-value⑧"></a>
>
> The box on the right is a bit bigger than the one on the left, so the [aspect value](#aspect-value) of this font is something less than 0.5. Adjust the value until the boxes align.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font①"></a>

### <a id="font-prop"></a>2.7.  Shorthand font property: the [font](#propdef-font) property

| Field               | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|---------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="propdef-font"></a>font                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#value-defs">Value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-system-font-family-name-value③"></a><a id="ref-for-comb-one⑤⓪"></a><a id="ref-for-mult-comma①"></a><a id="ref-for-propdef-font-family②"></a><a id="ref-for-propdef-line-height③"></a><a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size①①"></a><a id="ref-for-mult-opt①"></a><a id="ref-for-font-width-css3-values"></a><a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight④"></a><a id="ref-for-font-variant-css21-values"></a><a id="ref-for-comb-any"></a><a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style③"></a>\[ \[ [\<'font-style'\>](#propdef-font-style) [\|\|](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#comb-any) [\<font-variant-css2\>](#font-variant-css21-values) <a id="ref-for-comb-any①"></a>\|\| [\<'font-weight'\>](#propdef-font-weight) <a id="ref-for-comb-any②"></a>\|\| [\<font-width-css3\>](#font-width-css3-values) \][?](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#mult-opt) [\<'font-size'\>](#propdef-font-size) \[ / [\<'line-height'\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS2/visudet.html#propdef-line-height) \]<a id="ref-for-mult-opt②"></a>? [\<'font-family'\>](#propdef-font-family)[\#](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#mult-comma) \] [\|](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#comb-one) [\<system-font-family-name\>](#system-font-family-name-value) |

Tests

- [inheritance.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/inheritance.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/inheritance.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/inheritance.html)
- [font-shorthand-serialization-001.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-serialization-001.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-serialization-001.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-serialization-001.html)
- [font-shorthand-serialization-font-stretch.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-serialization-font-stretch.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-serialization-font-stretch.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-serialization-font-stretch.html)
- [font-shorthand-serialization-prevention.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-serialization-prevention.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-serialization-prevention.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-serialization-prevention.html)
- [font-shorthand-subproperties-reset.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-subproperties-reset.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-subproperties-reset.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-shorthand-subproperties-reset.html)
- [quoted-generic-ignored.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/quoted-generic-ignored.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/quoted-generic-ignored.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/quoted-generic-ignored.html)
- [font-computed.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-computed.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-computed.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-computed.html)
- [font-invalid.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-invalid.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-invalid.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-invalid.html)
- [font-valid.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-valid.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-valid.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-valid.html)
- [font-shorthand.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/variations/font-shorthand.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/variations/font-shorthand.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/variations/font-shorthand.html)

The syntax of this property is based on a traditional typographical shorthand notation to set multiple properties related to fonts. For historical and backwards-compatibility reasons, it is <em>almost</em> a shorthand property.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size①②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-line-height④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-family③"></a>

The [font](#propdef-font) property is, except as described below, a shorthand property for setting [font-style](#propdef-font-style), [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant), [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight), [font-width](#propdef-font-width), [font-size](#propdef-font-size), [line-height](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS2/visudet.html#propdef-line-height), [font-family](#propdef-font-family) at the same place in the stylesheet.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font③"></a>

Values for the [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) property can also be included but only those supported in CSS 2.1; none of the <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant③"></a>font-variant values added in CSS Fonts Levels 3 or 4 can be used in the [font](#propdef-font) shorthand:

<a id="font-variant-css21-values"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-variant-css21-values①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑤①"></a>

```text
<font-variant-css2>
    = normal | small-caps
```
<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font④"></a>

Values for the [font-width](#propdef-font-width) property can also be included but only those supported in CSS Fonts level 3, none of the <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width⑧"></a>font-width values added in this specification can be used in the [font](#propdef-font) shorthand:

<a id="font-width-css3-values"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-width-css3-values①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑤②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑤③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑤④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑤⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑤⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑤⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑤⑧"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑤⑨"></a>

```text
<font-width-css3>
    = normal | ultra-condensed | extra-condensed | condensed |
    semi-condensed | semi-expanded | expanded | extra-expanded |
    ultra-expanded
```
<a id="ref-for-propdef-font⑤"></a>

Therefore we have the following classification of font-related properties and their interaction with the [font](#propdef-font) property:

Set Explicitly  
<a id="ref-for-propdef-font⑥"></a>

These longhand properties may also be set using the [font](#propdef-font) shorthand property:

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-family④"></a>

  [font-family](#propdef-font-family)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size①③"></a>

  [font-size](#propdef-font-size)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width⑨"></a>

  [font-width](#propdef-font-width)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-stretch③"></a>

  [font-stretch](#propdef-font-stretch) (css3)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style⑤"></a>

  [font-style](#propdef-font-style)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-caps"></a>

  [font-variant-caps](#propdef-font-variant-caps) (css2)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight⑥"></a>

  [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-line-height⑤"></a>

  [line-height](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS2/visudet.html#propdef-line-height)

Reset Implicitly  
<a id="ref-for-reset-only-sub-property"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font⑦"></a>

These properties are a [reset-only sub-property](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#reset-only-sub-property) of the [font](#propdef-font) property and thus may not be set, but are reset to their initial values:

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings①"></a>

  [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-kerning"></a>

  [font-kerning](#propdef-font-kerning)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-language-override②"></a>

  [font-language-override](#propdef-font-language-override)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-optical-sizing①"></a>

  [font-optical-sizing](#propdef-font-optical-sizing)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust①⑤"></a>

  [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-alternates"></a>

  [font-variant-alternates](#propdef-font-variant-alternates)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-caps①"></a>

  [font-variant-caps](#propdef-font-variant-caps) (css3 and above)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-east-asian"></a>

  [font-variant-east-asian](#propdef-font-variant-east-asian)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-emoji"></a>

  [font-variant-emoji](#propdef-font-variant-emoji)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-ligatures"></a>

  [font-variant-ligatures](#propdef-font-variant-ligatures)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-numeric"></a>

  [font-variant-numeric](#propdef-font-variant-numeric)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-position①"></a>

  [font-variant-position](#propdef-font-variant-position)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings①"></a>

  [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings)

Cascaded Independently  
<a id="ref-for-longhand"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font⑧"></a>

These (despite the property name starting with "font-") are not [longhand properties](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#longhand) of the [font](#propdef-font) and thus are neither set nor reset by the <a id="ref-for-propdef-font⑨"></a>font property:

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-palette"></a>

  [font-palette](#propdef-font-palette)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-synthesis-weight"></a>

  [font-synthesis-weight](#propdef-font-synthesis-weight)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-synthesis-style①"></a>

  [font-synthesis-style](#propdef-font-synthesis-style)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-synthesis-small-caps"></a>

  [font-synthesis-small-caps](#propdef-font-synthesis-small-caps)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-synthesis-position"></a>

  [font-synthesis-position](#propdef-font-synthesis-position)

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font①⓪"></a>

All subproperties of the [font](#propdef-font) property in the Set Explicitly and Reset Implicitly groups are first reset to their initial values.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font①①"></a>

Then, those properties the Set Explicitly group that are given explicit values in the [font](#propdef-font) shorthand are set to those values. For a definition of allowed and initial values, see the individual longhand property definitions.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-font-shorthand"></a>
>
> ```text
> p { font: 12pt/14pt sans-serif }
> p { font: 80% sans-serif }
> p { font: x-large/110% "new century schoolbook", serif }
> p { font: bold italic large Palatino, serif }
> p { font: normal small-caps 120%/120% fantasy }
> p { font: condensed oblique 12pt "Helvetica Neue", serif; }
> p { font: condensed oblique 25deg 753 12pt "Helvetica Neue", serif; }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size①④"></a>
>
> In the second rule, the font size percentage value ("80%") refers to the computed [font-size](#propdef-font-size) of the parent element. In the third rule, the line height percentage ("110%") refers to the font size of the element itself.
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant④"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight⑦"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-font-variant-normal-value"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-weight-bold"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style⑥"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-style-italic②"></a>
>
> The first three rules do not specify the [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) and [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight) explicitly, so these properties receive their initial values ([normal](#font-variant-normal-value)). Notice that the font family name "new century schoolbook", which contains spaces, is enclosed in quotes. The fourth rule sets the <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight⑧"></a>font-weight to [bold](#valdef-font-weight-bold), the [font-style](#propdef-font-style) to [italic](#valdef-font-style-italic), and implicitly sets <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant⑤"></a>font-variant to <a id="ref-for-font-variant-normal-value①"></a>normal.
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant⑥"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-variant-caps-small-caps"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size①⑤"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-line-height⑥"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-family⑤"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-fantasy①"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style⑦"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight⑨"></a>
>
> The fifth rule sets the [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) ([small-caps](#valdef-font-variant-caps-small-caps)), the [font-size](#propdef-font-size) (120% of the parent’s font size), the [line-height](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS2/visudet.html#propdef-line-height) (120% of the font size) and the [font-family](#propdef-font-family) ([fantasy](#valdef-font-family-fantasy)). It follows that the keyword normal applies to the two remaining properties: [font-style](#propdef-font-style) and [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight).
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style⑧"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width①⓪"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size①⑥"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-family⑥"></a>
>
> The sixth rule sets the [font-style](#propdef-font-style), [font-width](#propdef-font-width), [font-size](#propdef-font-size), and [font-family](#propdef-font-family), the other font properties being set to their initial values.
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style⑨"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight①⓪"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width①①"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-width-condensed②"></a>
>
> The seventh rule sets [font-style](#propdef-font-style) to oblique 25deg, [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight) to 753, and [font-width](#propdef-font-width) to [condensed](#valdef-font-width-condensed). Note that the 25deg in this rule must be immediately following the "oblique" keyword.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width①②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font①②"></a>

Since the [font-width](#propdef-font-width) property was not defined in CSS 2.1, when using <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width①③"></a>font-width values within [font](#propdef-font) rules, authors should include a extra version compatible with older user agents:

```text
p {
  font: 80% sans-serif;   /* for older user agents */
  font: condensed 80% sans-serif;
}
```
<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-caption①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-small-caption①"></a>

System fonts can only be set as a whole; that is, the font family, size, weight, style, etc. are all set at the same time. These values can then be altered individually if desired. If no font with the indicated characteristics exists on a given platform, the user agent should either intelligently substitute (e.g., a smaller version of the [caption](#valdef-font-caption) font might be used for the [small-caption](#valdef-font-small-caption) font), or substitute a user agent default font. As for regular fonts, if, for a system font, any of the individual properties are not part of the operating system’s available user preferences, those properties should be set to their initial values.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-family⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font①③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight①①"></a>

That is why this property is "almost" a shorthand property: system fonts can only be specified with this property, not with [font-family](#propdef-font-family) itself, so [font](#propdef-font) allows authors to do more than the sum of its subproperties. However, the individual properties such as [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight) are still given values taken from the system font, which can be independently varied.

Note that the keywords used for the system fonts listed above are only treated as keywords when they occur in the initial position, in other positions the same string is treated as part of the font family name:

```text
font: menu;        /* use the font settings for system menus */
font: large menu;  /* use a font family named "menu" */
```
> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-menu-font"></a>
>
> ```text
> button { font: 300 italic 1.3em/1.7em "FB Armada", sans-serif }
> button p { font: menu }
> button p em { font-weight: bolder }
> ```
>
> If the font used for dropdown menus on a particular system happened to be, for example, 9-point Charcoal, with a weight of 600, then P elements that were descendants of BUTTON would be displayed as if this rule were in effect:
>
> ```text
> button p { font: 600 9pt Charcoal }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font①④"></a>
>
> Because the [font](#propdef-font) shorthand resets to its initial value any property not explicitly given a value, this has the same effect as this declaration:
>
> ```text
> button p {
>   font-style: normal;
>   font-variant: normal;
>   font-weight: 600;
>   font-size: 9pt;
>   line-height: normal;
>   font-family: Charcoal
> }
> ```
### <a id="font-synthesis-intro"></a>2.8.  Controlling synthetic faces

Synthetic font faces are a fallback strategy to provide an approximation to the requested font when a font family lacks a suitable face.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-synthesis-types"></a> For example, a user agent might:
>
> - synthesize a bold face by drawing a thin stroke around each glyph
>
> - synthesize small-caps, superscripts, or subscripts by rendering regular glyphs at a smaller size and positioning them appropriately
>
> - synthesize an oblique face by geometrical shearing of each glyph

Synthesis is a fallback strategy because the end result, while perhaps better than nothing at all, does not meet the level of quality of a face designed for the purpose.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-synthesis③"></a>

Variable fonts, where the font designer has provided one or more axes of variation do <em>not</em> count as font synthesis and their use is not affected by the [font-synthesis](#propdef-font-synthesis) property.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-synthesis-weight①"></a>

This property controls whether user agents are allowed to synthesize oblique font faces when a font family lacks oblique faces.

<a id="valdef-font-synthesis-style-auto"></a>auto  
Synthesis of oblique faces is allowed

<a id="valdef-font-synthesis-style-none"></a>none  
Synthesis of oblique faces is not allowed

<a id="valdef-font-synthesis-style-oblique-only"></a>oblique-only  
Synthesis of oblique faces is allowed, but they must not be used as fallback if italic is specified

<a id="vertical-oblique-synthesis"></a> In vertical text, for positive oblique angles, the glyph is skewed such that the line-over edge shifts towards the line-right side while the line-under edge shifts towards the line-left side. For negative oblique angles, the glyph is skewed such that the line-over edge shifts towards the line-left side while the line-under edge shifts towards the line-right side. Skewing is about the center of the glyph.

> <a id="ex-no-obliqued-arabic"></a> The style rule below disables the use of synthetically obliqued Arabic:

## <a id="font-rendering-controls"></a>3.  Font Rendering Controls

### <a id="font-rendering-controls-introduction"></a>3.1.  Introduction to Font Rendering Controls

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule"></a>

When using downloadable Web Fonts via [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule), the user agent needs to know what to do while the font is actively loading. Most web browsers have adopted some form of timeout:

| Browser           | Timeout   | Fallback | Swap |
|-------------------|-----------|----------|------|
| Chrome 35+        | 3 seconds | yes      | yes  |
| Opera             | 3 seconds | yes      | yes  |
| Firefox           | 3 seconds | yes      | yes  |
| Internet Explorer | 0 seconds | yes      | yes  |
| Safari            | 3 seconds | yes      | yes  |

- Chrome and Firefox have a 3 second timeout after which the text is shown with the fallback font. Eventually, a swap occurs: the text is re-rendered with the intended font once it becomes available.

- Internet Explorer has a 0 second timeout which results in immediate text rendering: if the requested font is not yet available, fallback is used, and text is rerendered later once the requested font becomes available.

While these default behaviors are reasonable, they’re unfortunately inconsistent across browsers. Worse, no single approach is sufficient to cover the range of use-cases required by modern user-experience– and performance–conscious applications.

The Font Loading API [\[CSS-FONT-LOADING-3\]](#biblio-css-font-loading-3) allows a developer to override some of the above behaviors, but that requires scripting, a non-trivial amount of effort, and ultimately doesn’t provide sufficient hooks to cover <em>all</em> reasonable cases. Additionally, the developer needs to either inline the loading script into their page or load an external library, introducing additional network latency before the fonts can be loaded and delaying text rendering.

Design/performance-conscious web developers have a good sense for the relative importance of a given Web Font for the intended user experience. This specification provides them the ability to control font timeout and rendering behavior. Specifically, it lets developers:

- Define the font display policy when text is ready to be painted: block, or paint with fallback.

- Define the font display policy once the desired font is available: rerender text with the new font, or leave it with the fallback.

- Define custom timeout values for each font.

- Define custom display and timeout policies per element.

### <a id="font-display-timeline"></a>3.2.  The Font Display Timeline

<a id="ref-for-font-block-period"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-swap-period"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-failure-period"></a>

At the moment the user agent first attempts to use a given downloaded font face on a page, the font face’s <a id="font-download-timer"></a>font download timer is started. This timer advances through three periods of time associated with the font face—​the [block period](#font-block-period), the [swap period](#font-swap-period), and the [failure period](#font-failure-period)—​which dictate the rendering behavior of any elements using the font face:

- <a id="ref-for-render-with-an-invisible-fallback-font-face"></a>

  <a id="ref-for-font-block-period①"></a>

  The first period is the <a id="font-block-period"></a>font block period. During this period, if the font face is not loaded, any element attempting to use it must instead [render with an invisible fallback font face](#render-with-an-invisible-fallback-font-face). If the font face successfully loads during the [block period](#font-block-period), the font face is then used normally.

- <a id="ref-for-font-block-period②"></a>

  <a id="ref-for-render-with-a-fallback-font-face"></a>

  <a id="ref-for-font-swap-period①"></a>

  The second period, occurring immediately after the [block period](#font-block-period), is the <a id="font-swap-period"></a>font swap period. During this period, if the font face is not loaded, any element attempting to use it must instead [render with a fallback font face](#render-with-a-fallback-font-face). If the font face successfully loads during the [swap period](#font-swap-period), the font face is then used normally.

- <a id="ref-for-font-swap-period②"></a>

  The third period, occurring immediately after the [swap period](#font-swap-period), is the <a id="font-failure-period"></a>font failure period. If the font face is not yet loaded when this period starts, it’s marked as a failed load, causing normal font fallback. Otherwise, the font face is used normally.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-family⑧"></a>

To <a id="render-with-a-fallback-font-face"></a>render with a fallback font face for a given element, the user agent must find the first font face specified in the element’s [font-family](#propdef-font-family) list which is already loaded, and use that for rendering text. Doing this must not trigger loads of any of the fallback fonts.

<a id="ref-for-render-with-a-fallback-font-face①"></a>

To <a id="render-with-an-invisible-fallback-font-face"></a>render with an invisible fallback font face for a given element, find a font face as per "[render with a fallback font face](#render-with-a-fallback-font-face)". Create an anonymous font face with the same metrics as the selected font face but with all glyphs "invisible" (containing no "ink"), and use that for rendering text. Doing this must not trigger loads of any of the fallback fonts.

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-face-font-display-fallback"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-face-font-display-optional"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="issue">Issue</strong>
>
> <a id="issue-160eca9c"></a> [fallback](#valdef-font-face-font-display-fallback) and [optional](#valdef-font-face-font-display-optional) can result in some faces in a family being used while others are required to fallback, giving a "ransom note" look. Perhaps require that all fonts in a family have the same behavior (all swapped in, or all fallback)? See also the @font-feature-values for controlling the behavior on a font family basis.

## <a id="font-resources"></a>4.  Font Resources

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule①"></a>

### <a id="font-face-rule"></a>4.1.  The <a id="at-font-face-rule"></a>[@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule②"></a>

The [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule allows for linking to fonts that are automatically fetched and activated when needed. This allows authors to select a font that closely matches the design goals for a given page rather than limiting the font choice to a set of fonts available on a given platform. A set of font descriptors define the location of a font resource, either locally or externally, along with the style characteristics of an individual face. Multiple <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule③"></a>@font-face rules can be used to construct font families with a variety of faces. Using CSS font matching rules, a user agent can selectively download only those faces that are needed for a given piece of text.

Its syntax is:

<a id="ref-for-typedef-declaration-list"></a>

```text
@font-face {
  <declaration-list>
}
```
Tests

- [downloadable-font-in-iframe-print.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/downloadable-font-in-iframe-print.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/downloadable-font-in-iframe-print.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/downloadable-font-in-iframe-print.html)
- [downloadable-font-print.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/downloadable-font-print.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/downloadable-font-print.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/downloadable-font-print.html)
- [remove-loaded-font-face-rule.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/remove-loaded-font-face-rule.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/remove-loaded-font-face-rule.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/remove-loaded-font-face-rule.html)
- [at-font-face-descriptors.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/variations/at-font-face-descriptors.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/variations/at-font-face-descriptors.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/variations/at-font-face-descriptors.html)

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule④"></a>

The [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule accepts the descriptors defined in this specification.

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑤"></a>

Each [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule)rule specifies a value for every font descriptor, either implicitly or explicitly. Those not given explicit values in the rule take the initial value listed with each descriptor in this specification. These descriptors apply solely within the context of the <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑥"></a>@font-face rule in which they are defined, and do not apply to document language elements. There is no notion of which elements the descriptors apply to or whether the values are inherited by child elements. When a given descriptor occurs multiple times in a given <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑦"></a>@font-face rule, only the last descriptor declaration is used and all prior declarations for that descriptor are ignored.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-gentium"></a> To use a downloadable font called Gentium:
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
> font-family: Gentium;
> src: url(http://example.com/fonts/Gentium.woff);
> }
> 
> p { font-family: Gentium, serif; }
> ```
>
> The user agent will download Gentium and use it when rendering text within paragraph elements. If for some reason the site serving the font is unavailable, the default serif font will be used.

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑧"></a>

A given set of [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rules define a set of fonts available for use within the documents that contain these rules. When font matching is done, fonts defined using these rules are considered before other available fonts on a system.

Downloaded fonts are only available to documents that reference them. The process of activating these fonts must not make them available to other applications or to documents that don’t directly link to the same font. User agent implementers might consider it convenient to use downloaded fonts when rendering characters in other documents for which no other available font exists as part of the <em>installed font fallback</em> procedure. However, this would cause a security leak since the contents of one page would be able to affect other pages, something an attacker could use as an attack vector. These restrictions do not affect caching behavior, fonts are cached the same way other web resources are cached.

Tests

- [downloadable-font-scoped-to-document.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/downloadable-font-scoped-to-document.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/downloadable-font-scoped-to-document.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/downloadable-font-scoped-to-document.html)
- [web-font-no-longer-accessible-when-stylesheet-removed.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/web-font-no-longer-accessible-when-stylesheet-removed.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/web-font-no-longer-accessible-when-stylesheet-removed.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/web-font-no-longer-accessible-when-stylesheet-removed.html)

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑨"></a>

<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-family"></a>

<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-src"></a>

This at-rule follows the forward-compatible parsing rules of CSS. Like properties in a declaration block, declarations of any descriptors that are not supported by the user agent must be ignored. [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rules require a [font-family](#descdef-font-face-font-family) and [src](#descdef-font-face-src) descriptor; if either of these are missing, the <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule①⓪"></a>@font-face rule must not be considered when performing the [font matching algorithm](#font-matching-algorithm).

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule①①"></a>

In cases where user agents have limited platform resources or implement the ability to disable downloadable font resources, [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rules must simply be ignored; the behavior of individual descriptors as defined in this specification should not be altered.

<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-family①"></a>

### <a id="font-family-desc"></a>4.2. Font family: the [font-family](#descdef-font-face-font-family) descriptor

| Field               | Definition                                                         |
|---------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="descdef-font-face-font-family"></a>font-family                                     |
| <strong>For:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule①②"></a>[@font-face](#at-font-face-rule)                |
| <strong>Value:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value⑧"></a>[\<font-family-name\>](#font-family-name-value) |
| <strong>Initial:&#xA;      </strong> | N/A                                                                |

This descriptor defines the font family name that will be used in all CSS font family name matching. It overrides the font family names contained in the underlying font data. If the font family name is the same as a font family available in a given user’s environment, it effectively hides the underlying font for documents that use the stylesheet. This permits a web author to freely choose font-family names without worrying about conflicts with font family names present in a given user’s environment. Likewise, platform substitutions for a given font family name must not be used.

<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-src①"></a>

### <a id="src-desc"></a>4.3. Font reference: the [src](#descdef-font-face-src) descriptor

| Field               | Definition                                                     |
|---------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="descdef-font-face-src"></a>src                                         |
| <strong>For:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule①③"></a>[@font-face](#at-font-face-rule)            |
| <strong>Value:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-typedef-font-src-list"></a>[\<font-src-list\>](#typedef-font-src-list) |
| <strong>Initial:&#xA;      </strong> | N/A                                                            |

This descriptor specifies the resource containing font data. Its value is a prioritized, comma-separated list of external references or locally-installed font face names. When a font is needed the user agent iterates over the set of references listed, using the first one it can successfully parse and activate. Parsing this descriptor is more complicated than parsing other descriptors; see [§ 4.3.1 Parsing the src descriptor](#font-face-src-parsing) for the parsing rules. Activation of a font involves downloading the file or reading it from disk, parsing it, and perhaps additional user-agent-dependent steps. Fonts containing invalid data or local font faces that are not found are ignored and the user agent loads the next font in the list.

<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-src②"></a>

#### <a id="font-face-src-parsing"></a>4.3.1. Parsing the [src](#descdef-font-face-src) descriptor

<a id="ref-for-typedef-font-src-list①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-css-parse-a-comma-separated-list-according-to-a-css-grammar"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-font-src"></a>

To parse a <a id="typedef-font-src-list"></a>[\<font-src-list\>](#typedef-font-src-list) production, [parse a list](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-syntax-3/#css-parse-a-comma-separated-list-according-to-a-css-grammar) of [\<font-src\>](#typedef-font-src)s.

<a id="typedef-font-src"></a>

<a id="ref-for-url-value"></a>

<a id="format()"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-format-values"></a>

<a id="ref-for-mult-opt③"></a>

<a id="tech()"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-tech-values"></a>

<a id="ref-for-mult-comma②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-mult-opt④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑥⑥"></a>

<a id="local()"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value⑨"></a>

```text
<font-src> = <url> [ format( <font-format> ) ]? [ tech( <font-tech># ) ]?
  | local( <font-family-name> )
```
<a id="font-format-values"></a>

<a id="ref-for-string-value②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑥⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑥⑧"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑥⑨"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑦⓪"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑦①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑦②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑦③"></a>

```text
<font-format>
    = [ <string> | collection | embedded-opentype | opentype
      | svg | truetype | woff | woff2 ]
```
<a id="font-tech-values"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-features-tech-values"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑦④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑦⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑦⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one⑦⑦"></a>

```text
<font-tech>
    = [ <font-features-tech> | <color-font-tech>
      | variations | palettes | incremental ]
```

```text
    = [ features-opentype | features-aat | features-graphite ]
```

- [font-face-src-list.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-face-src-list.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-face-src-list.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-face-src-list.html)
- [font-face-src-local.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-face-src-local.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-face-src-local.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-face-src-local.html)
- [font-face-src-tech.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-face-src-tech.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-face-src-tech.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-face-src-tech.html)

While keywords are preferred to identify font formats, for reasons of backwards compatibility the following strings are also accepted, and have the same effect as if the equivalent modern syntax had been used.

|                               |                                   |
|-------------------------------|-----------------------------------|
| String form                   | Equivalent syntax                 |
| format("woff2")               | format(woff2)                     |
| format("woff")                | format(woff)                      |
| format("truetype")            | format(truetype)                  |
| format("opentype")            | format(opentype)                  |
| format("collection")          | format(collection)                |
| format("woff2-variations")    | format(woff2) tech(variations)    |
| format("woff-variations")     | format(woff) tech(variations)     |
| format("truetype-variations") | format(truetype) tech(variations) |
| format("opentype-variations") | format(opentype) tech(variations) |

Tests

- [format-specifiers-variations.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/format-specifiers-variations.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/format-specifiers-variations.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/format-specifiers-variations.html)

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: The CSS WG does not anticipate extending this list of format strings in the future.

If a component value is parsed correctly and is of a [font format](#font-format-definitions) or [font tech](#font-tech-definitions) that the UA supports, add it to the list of supported sources. If parsing a component value results in a parsing error or its format or tech are unsupported, do not add it to the list of supported sources.

<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-src③"></a>

If there are no supported entries at the end of this process, the value for the [src](#descdef-font-face-src) descriptor is a parse error.

These parsing rules allow for graceful fallback of fonts for user agents which don’t support a particular font tech or font format.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-incremental"></a> For example, when incremental transfer is not supported, a woff2 compressed version of the font is supplied, for optimal performance. Then, for [incremental transfer](https://w3c.github.io/IFT/Overview.html), the raw uncompressed OpenType font is provided, portions of which will be loaded on demand.
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: "MyIncrementallyLoadedWebFont";
>   src: url("FallbackURLForBrowsersWhichDontSupportIncrementalLoading.woff2") format("woff2");
>   src: url("MyIncrementallyLoadedWebFont.otf") format(opentype)  tech(incremental);
> }
> ```
<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-src④"></a>

#### <a id="font-face-src-loading"></a>4.3.2. Loading an individual item in the [src](#descdef-font-face-src) descriptor

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule①④"></a>

As with other URLs in CSS, the URL can be relative, in which case it is resolved relative to the location of the style sheet containing the [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule. In the case of SVG fonts, the URL points to an element within a document containing SVG font definitions. If the element reference is omitted, a reference to the first defined font is implied. Similarly, font container formats that can contain more than one font must load one and only one of the fonts for a given <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule①⑤"></a>@font-face rule. Fragment identifiers are used to indicate which font to load; these use the PostScript name of the font as defined in [\[RFC8081\]](#biblio-rfc8081).

Tests

- [font-opentype-collections.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/variations/font-opentype-collections.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/variations/font-opentype-collections.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/variations/font-opentype-collections.html)

Conformant user agents must not use a face from the resource unless its PostScript name exactly matches the fragment identifier, and must skip downloading the resource if the given fragment identifier cannot be a valid PostScript name.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-load-font-fragment"></a>
>
> ```text
> src: url(fonts/simple.woff);       /* load simple.woff relative to stylesheet location */
> src: url(/fonts/simple.woff);      /* load simple.woff from absolute location */
> src: url(fonts/coll.otc#foo);      /* load font foo from collection coll.otc
> src: url(fonts/coll.woff2#foo);    /* load font foo from woff2 collection coll.woff2
> src: url(fonts.svg#simple);        /* load SVG font with id 'simple' */
> ```
<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-src⑤"></a>

#### <a id="font-face-src-formats"></a>4.3.3. Selecting items in the [src](#descdef-font-face-src)

External references consist of a URL, followed by an optional hint describing the format of the font resource referenced by that URL. Conformant user agents must skip downloading a font resource if the format hint indicates an unsupported or unknown font format, or if any of the font technologies are unsupported by the user agent. If no format hint is supplied, the user agent should download the font resource.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-woff2-woff1"></a> For example, the following shows how to load a WOFF 2 font if possible, otherwise a WOFF 1, otherwise use an OpenType font
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: bodytext;
>   src: url(ideal-sans-serif.woff2) format("woff2"),
>     url(ideal-sans-serif.woff) format("woff"),
>     url(basic-sans-serif.ttf) format("opentype");
> }
> ```
> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-fictitious-zebra"></a> In this example, if woff2 is not supported, an unsupported and fictitious "zebra" format is skipped and the opentype font is downloaded:
>
> ```text
> src: url(ideal.woff2) format("woff2"),
>     url(unsupported.zeb) format("zebra"),
>     url(basic.ttf) format("opentype");
> ```
> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-instance-in-collection"></a> Here an individual instance in a collection is loaded, or if collections are not supported, download an individual font instead.
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: 源ノ角ゴシック Code JP;
>   src: url(SourceHanCodeJP.otc#Regular) format("collection"),
>     url(SourceHanCodeJP-Regular.ttf) format("opentype");
> }
> ```
##### <a id="local-font-fallback"></a>4.3.3.1. Local font fallback

<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value①⓪"></a>

<a id="ref-for-css-wide-keywords④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-all-inherit①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-generic-font-family①⓪"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-family-serif⑨"></a>

When authors would prefer to use a locally available copy of a given font and download it if it’s not, `local()` can be used. The locally-installed [\<font-family-name\>](#font-family-name-value) argument to `local()` is a format-specific string that uniquely identifies a single font face within a larger family. The name can optionally be enclosed in quotes. If unquoted, the [unquoted font family name processing conventions](#font-family-name-syntax) apply; in other words, the name must be a sequence of identifiers separated by [whitespace](https://www.w3.org/TR/css3-syntax/#whitespace) which is converted to a string by joining the identifiers together separated by a single space; and thus, [CSS-wide keywords](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#css-wide-keywords) such as [inherit](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#valdef-all-inherit), and [\<generic-font-family\>](#typedef-generic-font-family) keywords such as [serif](#valdef-font-family-serif) are not allowed inside `local()`.

```css
/* regular face of Gentium */
@font-face {
  font-family: MyGentium;
  src: local(Gentium),    /* prefer locally available Gentium */
    url(Gentium.woff);  /* otherwise, download it */
}
```
> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-local-no-keywords"></a> For example, this use of `local()` would be an error:
>
> ```css
> @font-face {
>   font-family: foo;
>   src: local(inherit);
> }
> ```
For OpenType and TrueType fonts, this string is used to match only the Postscript name or the full font name in the name table of locally available fonts. Which type of name is used varies by platform and font, so authors should include both of these names to assure proper matching across platforms. Platform substitutions for a given font name must not be used.

```text
/* bold face of Gentium */
@font-face {
  font-family: MyGentium;
  src: local(Gentium Bold),    /* full font name */
    local(Gentium-Bold),    /* Postscript name */
    url(GentiumBold.woff);  /* otherwise, download it */
  font-weight: bold;
}
```
<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule①⑥"></a>

Just as an [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule specifies the characteristics of a single font within a family, the unique name used with `local()` specifies a single font, not an entire font family. Defined in terms of OpenType font data, the Postscript name is found in the font’s [name table](https://www.microsoft.com/typography/otspec/name.htm), in the name record with nameID = 6 (see [\[OPENTYPE\]](#biblio-opentype) for more details). The Postscript name is the commonly used key for all fonts on OSX and for Postscript CFF fonts under Windows. The full font name (nameID = 4) is used as a unique key for fonts with TrueType glyphs on Windows.

For OpenType fonts with multiple localizations of the full font name, the US English version must be used (language ID = 0x409 for Windows and language ID = 0 for Macintosh) or the first localization when a US English full font name is not available (the OpenType specification recommends that [all fonts minimally include US English names](https://www.microsoft.com/typography/otspec/recom.htm)). User agents that also match other full font names, e.g. matching the Dutch name when the current system locale is set to Dutch, are considered non-conformant.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: This is done, not to prefer English, but to avoid matching inconsistencies across font versions and OS localizations, since font style names (e.g. "Bold") are frequently localized into many languages and the set of localizations available varies widely across platform and font version.

User agents that match a concatenation of family name (nameID = 1) with style name (nameID = 2) are considered non-conformant.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: This also allows for referencing faces that belong to larger families that cannot otherwise be referenced.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-local-font"></a> Use a local font or reference an SVG font in another document:
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: Headline;
>   src: local(Futura-Medium),
>     url(images/fonts.svg#MyGeometricModern) format("svg");
> }
> ```
>
> Create an alias for local Japanese fonts on different platforms:
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: jpgothic;
>   src: local(HiraKakuPro-W3), local(Meiryo), local(IPAPGothic);
> }
> ```
>
> Reference a font face that cannot be matched within a larger family:
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: Hoefler Text Ornaments;
>   /* has the same font properties as Hoefler Text Regular */
>   src: local(HoeflerText-Ornaments);
> }
> ```
>
> Since localized fullnames never match, a document with the header style rules below would always render using the default serif font, regardless whether a particular system locale parameter is set to Finnish or not:
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: SectionHeader;
>   src: local("Arial Lihavoitu");  /* Finnish fullname for Arial Bold, should fail */
>   font-weight: bold;
> }
> 
> h2 { font-family: SectionHeader, serif; }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-src⑥"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule①⑦"></a>
>
> A conformant user agent would never load the font 'gentium.eot' in the example below, since it is included in the first definition of the [src](#descdef-font-face-src) descriptor which is overridden by the second definition in the same [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule:
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: MainText;
>   src: url(gentium.eot);                     /* for use with older user agents */
>   src: local("Gentium"), url(gentium.woff);  /* Overrides src definition */
> }
> ```
<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-style"></a>

<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-weight"></a>

<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-width"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule②①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-weight-bolder①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-weight-lighter①"></a>

These descriptors define the characteristics of a font face and are used in the process of matching styles to specific faces. For a font family defined with several [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rules, user agents can either download all faces in the family or use these descriptors to selectively download font faces that match actual styles used in document. The meaning of the values for these descriptors are the same as those for the corresponding font properties except that relative keywords are not allowed, [bolder](#valdef-font-weight-bolder) and [lighter](#valdef-font-weight-lighter). If these descriptors are omitted, initial values are assumed. If specified values are out of range of the accepted values of the property of the same name, the descriptor is treated as a parse error.

Ranges are accepted in these three descriptors in place of a single value. Where a single value is specified, it has the same meaning as a range with identical startpoint and endpoint. User agents must swap the computed value of the startpoint and endpoint of the range in order to forbid decreasing ranges. Both endpoints are inclusive. The ranges are used in the [Font Matching Algorithm](#font-matching-algorithm) below.

Tests

- [font-face-range-order.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-face-range-order.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-face-range-order.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-face-range-order.html)
- [range-descriptor-reversed.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/matching/range-descriptor-reversed.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/matching/range-descriptor-reversed.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/matching/range-descriptor-reversed.html)

The auto values for these three descriptors have the following effects:

- <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-style-normal①"></a>

  <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-weight-normal"></a>

  <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-width-normal"></a>

  For font selection purposes, the font is selected as if the appropriate normal value ([normal](#valdef-font-style-normal), [normal](#valdef-font-weight-normal) or [normal](#valdef-font-width-normal)) is chosen

- For variation axis clamping, clamping does not occur

The value for these font face style attributes is used in place of the style implied by the underlying font data. This allows authors to combine faces in flexible combinations, even in situations where the original font data was arranged differently. User agents that implement synthetic bolding and obliquing must only apply synthetic styling in cases where the font descriptors imply this is needed, rather than based on the style attributes implied by the font data. However, variation values applied to fonts defined with '@font-face' will be clamped to both the values specified in these descriptors, or implied by the application of variation parameters (such as manipulation of the `wght` axis to satisfy the requested font-weight), as well as the values supported by the font file itself.

Tests

- [synthetic-bold-out-of-capabilities-range.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/synthetic-bold-out-of-capabilities-range.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/synthetic-bold-out-of-capabilities-range.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/synthetic-bold-out-of-capabilities-range.html)

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule②②"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-descriptors-matching"></a> The font descriptors defined in this section are used for selecting a font from within the set of fonts defined by [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rules for a given family.
>
> Consider a family containing a single, regular face:
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: BaskervilleSimple;
>   src: url(baskerville-regular.woff2);
> }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule②③"></a>
>
> Unstyled text would display using the regular face defined in the [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule:
>
> ![regular face display](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/fiddlesticks-regular.png)
>
> However, italic text would display in most user agents using synthetically obliqued glyphs from the regular face, since a separate italic face is not defined:
>
> ![synthetic italics display](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/fiddlesticks-synitalics.png)
>
> Now consider a family for which an actual italic face is defined:
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: BaskervilleFull;
>   src: url(baskerville-regular.woff2);
> }
> 
> @font-face {
>   font-family: BaskervilleFull;
>   src: url(baskerville-italic.woff2);
>   font-style: italic;
> }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule②④"></a>
>
> The second [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule defines the font resource `baskerville-italic.woff` to have style attributes of normal weight, normal stretch and italic style. When displaying italic text, the user agent will use this font, since it’s the closest match for italic text. Thus, the text will display using glyphs designed by a type designer rather than using synthetically obliqued glyphs from the regular face:
>
> ![real italics display](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/fiddlesticks-italics.png)
>
> See the section on [font matching](#font-style-matching) for more complete details of the process used to select a particular face within a font family.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight①②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width①⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style①⓪"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-descriptors-range"></a> Fonts may advertise the range of [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight), [font-width](#propdef-font-width), and [font-style](#propdef-font-style) they are compatible with.
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: Lastima;
>   src: url(lastima-varfont.woff2);
>   font-weight: 100 399;
> }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule②⑤"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight①③"></a>
>
> The above [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule indicates that `lastima-varfont.woff` should be used when [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight) is between 100 and 399. Depending on if there are any other <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule②⑥"></a>@font-face rules which specify `font-family: Lastima`, `lastima-varfont.woff` might be used for values of <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight①④"></a>font-weight outside of the 100 - 399 range. For more details, see the [§ 5 Font Matching Algorithm](#font-matching-algorithm).
>
> <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule②⑦"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight①⑤"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width①⑥"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style①①"></a>
>
> As [above](#ex-descriptors-matching), multiple [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rules may be joined together into a single family, spanning multiple ranges of [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight), [font-width](#propdef-font-width), and/or [font-style](#propdef-font-style):
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: Lastima;
>   src: url(lastima-varfont-lightrange.woff2);
>   font-weight: 100 399;
> }
> @font-face {
>   font-family: Lastima;
>   src: url(lastima-varfont-heavyrange.woff2);
>   font-weight: 400 700;
> }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule②⑧"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight①⑥"></a>
>
> The above [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rules indicate that `lastima-varfont-lightrange.woff` should be used when [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight) is between 100 and 399, whereas `lastima-varfont-heavyrange.woff` should be used when <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight①⑦"></a>font-weight is between 400 and 700.

<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-stretch"></a>

#### <a id="font-stretch-desc"></a>4.4.1.  Font width: the [font-stretch](#descdef-font-face-font-stretch) legacy name alias

<a id="ref-for-legacy-name-alias①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-width①"></a>

For historical reasons, a <a id="descdef-font-face-font-stretch"></a>font-stretch descriptor exists which is a [legacy name alias](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#legacy-name-alias) and functions in the identical way to the [font-width](#descdef-font-face-font-width) descriptor.
