<a id="font-feature-index-value"></a>

<a id="ref-for-integer-value"></a>

```text
<font-feature-index> = <integer>
```
<a id="font-feature-value-name-value"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-ident"></a>

```text
<font-feature-value-name> = <ident>
```
For any given character, fonts can provide a variety of alternate glyphs in addition to the default glyph for that character. This property provides control over the selection of these alternate glyphs.

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-name-value⑥"></a>

For many of the property values listed below, several different alternate glyphs are available. How many alternates are available and what they represent is font-specific, so these are each marked <a id="font-specific"></a>font specific in the value definitions below. Because the nature of these alternates is font-specific, the [@font-feature-values](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values) rule is used to define values for a specific font family or set of families that associate a font-specific numeric [\<font-feature-index\>](#font-feature-index-value) with a custom [\<font-feature-value-name\>](#font-feature-value-name-value), which is then used in this property to select specific alternates:

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-noble-script"></a>
>
> ```text
> @font-feature-values Noble Script { @swash { swishy: 1; flowing: 2; } }
> 
> p {
>   font-family: Noble Script;
>   font-variant-alternates: swash(flowing); /* use swash alternate #2 */
> }
> ```
<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-name-value⑦"></a>

When a particular [\<font-feature-value-name\>](#font-feature-value-name-value) has not been defined for a given family or for a particular feature type, the computed value must be the same as if it had been defined. However, property values that contain these undefined <a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-name-value⑧"></a>\<font-feature-value-name\> identifiers must be ignored when choosing glyphs.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-effectively-same"></a>
>
> ```text
> /* these two style rules are effectively the same */
> 
> p { font-variant-alternates: swash(unknown-value); } /* not a defined value, ignored */
> p { font-variant-alternates: normal; }
> ```
This allows values to be defined and used for a given set of font families but ignored if fallback occurs, since the font family name would be different. If a given value is outside the range supported by a given font, the value is ignored. These values never apply to generic font families.

Individual values have the following meanings:

<a id="font-variant-alternates-normal-value"></a>normal

None of the features listed below are enabled.

<a id="valdef-font-variant-alternates-historical-forms"></a>historical-forms

Enables display of historical forms (OpenType feature: hist).

![historical form example](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/hist.png)

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-name-value⑨"></a>

<a id="stylistic"></a>stylistic([\<font-feature-value-name\>](#font-feature-value-name-value))

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-specific"></a>

Enables display of stylistic alternates ([font specific](#font-specific), OpenType feature: salt [\<font-feature-index\>](#font-feature-index-value)).

![stylistic alternate example](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/salt.png)

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-name-value①⓪"></a>

<a id="styleset"></a>styleset([\<font-feature-value-name\>](#font-feature-value-name-value)\#)

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-specific①"></a>

Enables display with stylistic sets ([font specific](#font-specific), OpenType feature: ss[\<font-feature-index\>](#font-feature-index-value) OpenType currently defines ss01 through ss20).

![styleset example](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/ssnn.png)

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-name-value①①"></a>

<a id="character-variant"></a>character-variant([\<font-feature-value-name\>](#font-feature-value-name-value)\#)

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-specific②"></a>

Enables display of specific character variants ([font specific](#font-specific), OpenType feature: cv[\<font-feature-index\>](#font-feature-index-value) OpenType currently defines cv01 through cv99).

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-name-value①②"></a>

<a id="swash"></a>swash([\<font-feature-value-name\>](#font-feature-value-name-value))

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-specific③"></a>

Enables display of swash glyphs ([font specific](#font-specific), OpenType feature: swsh [\<font-feature-index\>](#font-feature-index-value), cswh <a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value⑤"></a>\<font-feature-index\>).

![swash example](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/swsh.png)

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-name-value①③"></a>

<a id="ornaments"></a>ornaments([\<font-feature-value-name\>](#font-feature-value-name-value))

<a id="ref-for-ornaments"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-specific④"></a>

Enables replacement of default glyphs with ornaments, if provided in the font ([font specific](#font-specific), OpenType feature: ornm [\<font-feature-index\>](#font-feature-index-value)). Some fonts may offer ornament glyphs as alternates for a wide collection of characters; however, displaying arbitrary characters (e.g., alphanumerics) as ornaments is poor practice as it distorts the semantics of the data. Font designers are encouraged to encode all ornaments (except those explicitly encoded in the Unicode Dingbats blocks, etc.) as alternates for the bullet character (U+2022) to allow authors to select the desired glyph using [ornaments()](#ornaments).

![ornaments example](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/ornm.png)

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-name-value①④"></a>

<a id="annotation"></a>annotation([\<font-feature-value-name\>](#font-feature-value-name-value))

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-specific⑤"></a>

Enables display of alternate annotation forms ([font specific](#font-specific), OpenType feature: nalt [\<font-feature-index\>](#font-feature-index-value)).

![alternate annotation form example](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/nalt.png)

### <a id="font-feature-values"></a>6.9.  Defining font specific alternates: the <a id="at-ruledef-font-feature-values"></a>@font-feature-values rule

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-alternates②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-specific⑥"></a>

Several of the possible values of [font-variant-alternates](#propdef-font-variant-alternates) listed above are labeled as [font specific](#font-specific); a font can define not just a single glyph for the feature, but <em>multiple</em> possible glyph variants, and associate each one with a numeric index to let you choose which to turn on.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings④"></a>

These numeric indexes are idiosyncratic to each face; a `swsh 1` feature on one font face might turn on the swash version of capital Q, while on another font face it turns on the swash version of the &#x26;. Thus, specifying the index in [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings) requires that the author know <em>exactly</em> which font will be used on an element; if they get it wrong (due to font fallback selecting a different font) they might end up turning on an entirely different, and undesirable, feature to what they wanted! It also means that the author can’t easily turn on similar features for elements with different fonts; they have to individually set different <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings⑤"></a>font-feature-settings values for each that uses the correct numeric indexes for the desired features.

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values⑤"></a>

To fix this issue, the [@font-feature-values](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values) rule lets an author assign, for each font face, a human-friendly name to specific feature indexes. Using these friendly names, an author can easily turn on similar features regardless of the font in use (if they’ve defined that name for all the fonts), and be sure they’re not accidentally turning on unrelated features (as fonts without those names defined for them simply won’t do anything).

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-font-feature-values"></a>
>
> Using a commonly named value allows authors to use a single style rule to cover a set of fonts for which the underlying selector is different for each font. If either font in the example below is found, a circled number glyph will be used:
>
> ```text
> @font-feature-values Otaru Kisa {
>   @annotation { circled: 1; black-boxed: 3; }
> }
> @font-feature-values Taisho Gothic {
>   @annotation { boxed: 1; circled: 4; }
> }
> 
> h3.title {
>   /* circled form defined for both fonts */
>   font-family: Otaru Kisa, Taisho Gothic;
>   font-variant: annotation(circled);
> }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values⑥"></a>
>
> Trying to turn on the "circled" forms for either font explicitly, using [@font-feature-values](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values), would require the author know <em>for certain</em> which font will be used; if they expected "Otaru Kisa" and wrote font-feature-settings: "nalt" 1;, it would turn on "circled" characters in Otara Kisa, but would instead turn on <strong>boxed</strong> characters if the system fell back to Taisho Gothic, as that’s what Taisho Gothic associates with `nalt 1`!

#### <a id="font-feature-values-syntax"></a>6.9.1. Basic syntax

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-block"></a>

An [@font-feature-values](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values) rule’s prelude contains a list of font family names, followed by a block containing multiple <a id="font-feature-value-block"></a>feature value blocks, a special type of subsidiary at-rule. Each [feature value block](#font-feature-value-block) contains declarations mapping author-chosen human-friendly names (such as "flowing") to feature indexes for the associated feature.

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-block①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-alternates③"></a>

Each [font feature value](#font-feature-value-block) has the same meaning as the corresponding value of the [font-variant-alternates](#propdef-font-variant-alternates) property.

<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value①①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-mult-comma⑧"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-declaration-rule-list"></a>

<a id="typedef-font-feature-values-font-feature-value-type"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-stylistic"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①②⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-historical-forms"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①②⑧"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-styleset"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①②⑨"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-character-variant"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①③⓪"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-swash"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①③①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-ornaments"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①③②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-annotation"></a>

<a id="at-ruledef-font-feature-values-stylistic"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-declaration-list①"></a>

<a id="at-ruledef-font-feature-values-historical-forms"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-declaration-list②"></a>

<a id="at-ruledef-font-feature-values-styleset"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-declaration-list③"></a>

<a id="at-ruledef-font-feature-values-character-variant"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-declaration-list④"></a>

<a id="at-ruledef-font-feature-values-swash"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-declaration-list⑤"></a>

<a id="at-ruledef-font-feature-values-ornaments"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-declaration-list⑥"></a>

<a id="at-ruledef-font-feature-values-annotation"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-declaration-list⑦"></a>

```text
@font-feature-values = @font-feature-values <font-family-name># { <declaration-rule-list> }

<font-feature-value-type> = <@stylistic> | <@historical-forms> | <@styleset> | <@character-variant>
  | <@swash> | <@ornaments> | <@annotation>

@stylistic = @stylistic { <declaration-list> }
@historical-forms = @historical-forms { <declaration-list> }
@styleset = @styleset { <declaration-list> }
@character-variant = @character-variant { <declaration-list> }
@swash = @swash { <declaration-list> }
@ornaments = @ornaments { <declaration-list> }
@annotation = @annotation { <declaration-list> }
```
Tests

- [alternates-order.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/alternates-order.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/alternates-order.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/alternates-order.html)
- [test_font_feature_values_parsing.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/test_font_feature_values_parsing.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/test_font_feature_values_parsing.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/test_font_feature_values_parsing.html)

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values⑧"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value①②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-family①⑥"></a>

The [@font-feature-values](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values) prelude is a comma-delimited list of font family names that match the definition of [\<font-family-name\>](#font-family-name-value) for the [font-family](#propdef-font-family) property. This means that only named font families are allowed; rules that include generic or system fonts in the list of font families are syntax errors. However, if a user agent defines a generic font to be a specific named font (e.g. Helvetica), the settings associated with that family name will be used. If syntax errors occur within the <a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value①③"></a>\<font-family-name\> list, the entire rule <a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values⑨"></a>@font-feature-values rule is invalid and must be ignored.

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values①⓪"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-declaration-rule-list①"></a>

The [@font-feature-values](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values) block accepts [\<declaration-rule-list\>](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-syntax-3/#typedef-declaration-rule-list) as its contents; these list items are either:

- <a id="ref-for-at-rule"></a>

  <a id="ref-for-typedef-font-feature-values-font-feature-value-type"></a>

  [at-rules](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-syntax-3/#at-rule) named by one of the [\<font-feature-value-type\>](#typedef-font-feature-values-font-feature-value-type) at-keyword tokens

or

- <a id="ref-for-descdef-font-feature-values-font-display②"></a>

  the [font-display](#descdef-font-feature-values-font-display) descriptor.

<a id="ref-for-typedef-font-feature-values-font-feature-value-type①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-block②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-cssstyledeclaration-declarations"></a>

<a id="ref-for-css-css-identifier"></a>

<a id="ref-for-integer-value①"></a>

Specifying the same [\<font-feature-value-type\>](#typedef-font-feature-values-font-feature-value-type) more than once is valid; their contents are cascaded together. Each [feature value block](#font-feature-value-block) accepts a list of [declarations](https://www.w3.org/TR/cssom-1/#cssstyledeclaration-declarations), the <a id="font-feature-value-declaration"></a>font feature value declarations, where the declaration’s name can be any [css identifier](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#css-css-identifier), and the value must be a list of one or more non-negative [\<integer\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#integer-value)s.

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-block③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-name-value①⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-dfn-case-sensitive"></a>

The [feature value blocks](#font-feature-value-block) accept any declaration name; these names must be [\<font-feature-value-name\>](#font-feature-value-name-value), per standard CSS syntax rules, and are [case-sensitive](https://www.w3.org/TR/i18n-glossary/#dfn-case-sensitive) (so foo: 1; and FOO: 2 define two different features).

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-annotation①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-ornaments①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-stylistic①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-swash①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value⑧"></a>

Each declaration’s value in [@annotation](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values-annotation), [@ornaments](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values-ornaments), [@stylistic](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values-stylistic), [@swash](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values-swash), must match the grammar [\<font-feature-index\>](#font-feature-index-value), or else the declaration is invalid and must be ignored.

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-character-variant①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value⑨"></a>

Each declaration’s value in [@character-variant](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values-character-variant) must match the grammar [\<font-feature-index \[0,99\]\>](#font-feature-index-value) <a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value①⓪"></a>\<font-feature-index \[0,∞\]\>, or else the declaration is invalid and must be ignored.

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-styleset①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-index-value①①"></a>

Each declaration’s value in [@styleset](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values-styleset) must match the grammar [\<font-feature-index \[0,20\]\>](#font-feature-index-value)+, or else the declaration is invalid and must be ignored.

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-block④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values①①"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: Each feature name is unique only within a single [feature value block](#font-feature-value-block). Between different <a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-block⑤"></a>feature value blocks, or the same type of <a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-block⑥"></a>feature value blocks in separate [@font-feature-values](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values) rules, names can be reused without colliding.

<a id="ref-for-font-family-name-value①④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values①②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-declaration"></a>

<a id="ref-for-tuple"></a>

For each [\<font-family-name\>](#font-family-name-value) in the [@font-feature-values](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values) prelude, each [font feature value declaration](#font-feature-value-declaration) defines a mapping between a (family name, feature block name, declaration name) [tuple](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#tuple) and the list of one or more integers from the declaration’s value. If the same tuple appears more than once in a document (such as if a single block), the last-defined one is used.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-same-font-feature-values"></a> For example, the following all define the exact same set of font feature values:
>
> ```text
> /* Default */
> @font-feature-values foo {
>   @swash { pretty: 1; cool: 2; }
> }
> 
> /* Repeated declaration names */
> @font-feature-values foo {
>   @swash { pretty: 0; pretty: 1; cool: 2; }
> }
> 
> /* Multiple blocks of the same type */
> @font-feature-values foo {
>   @swash { pretty: 1; }
>   @swash { cool: 2; }
> }
> 
> /* Multiple rules for the same family */
> @font-feature-values foo {
>   @swash { pretty: 1; }
> }
> @font-feature-values foo {
>   @swash { cool: 2; }
> }
> ```
<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-declaration①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-font-feature-value-block⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values①③"></a>

A syntax error within a [font feature value declaration](#font-feature-value-declaration) makes the declaration invalid and ignored, but does not invalidate the [font feature value block](#font-feature-value-block) it occurs in. An unknown at-rule within a [@font-feature-values](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values) block (not using one of the predefined list of allowed at-keywords) makes that at-rule invalid and ignored, but does not invalidate the <a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values①④"></a>@font-feature-values rule.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-invalid-ignored"></a> Rules that are equivalent given syntax error handling:
>
> ```text
> @font-feature-values Bongo {
>   @swash { ornate: 1; }
>   annotation { boxed: 4; } /* should be @annotation! */
>   @swash { double-loops: 1; flowing: -1; } /* negative value */
>   @ornaments ; /* incomplete definition */
>   @styleset { double-W: 14; sharp-terminals: 16 1 } /* missing ; */
>   redrum  /* random editing mistake */
> }
> ```
>
> The example above is equivalent to:
>
> ```text
> @font-feature-values Bongo {
>   @swash { ornate: 1; }
>   @swash { double-loops: 1; }
>   @styleset { double-W: 14; sharp-terminals: 16 1; }
> }
> ```
<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values①⑤"></a>

If multiple [@font-feature-values](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values) rules are defined for a given family, the resulting values definitions are the union of the definitions contained within these rules. This allows a set of named values to be defined for a given font family globally for a site and specific additions made per-page.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-site-wide-per-page"></a> Using both site-wide and per-page feature values:
>
> site.css:
>
> ```text
> @font-feature-values Mercury Serif {
>   @styleset {
>     stacked-g: 3; /* "two-storey" versions of g, a */
>     stacked-a: 4;
>   }
> }
> ```
>
> page.css:
>
> ```text
> @font-feature-values Mercury Serif {
>   @styleset {
>   geometric-m: 7; /* alternate version of m */
>   }
> }
> 
> body {
>   font-family: Mercury Serif, serif;
> 
>   /* enable both the use of stacked g and alternate m */
>   font-variant-alternates: styleset(stacked-g, geometric-m);
> }
> ```
#### <a id="multi-value-features"></a>6.9.2.  Multi-valued feature value definitions

<a id="ref-for-font-specific⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-alternates④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-swash"></a>

Most [font specific](#font-specific) functional values of the [font-variant-alternates](#propdef-font-variant-alternates) property take a single value (e.g. [swash()](#swash)) which enables the feature.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-single-valued-feature"></a>
>
> ```text
> @font-feature-values Jupiter Serif {
>     @swash {
>       swishy: 5;     /* implies ss05 = 1 */
>       swirly: 2;     /* implies ss02 = 1 */
>     }
>   }
> ```
<a id="ref-for-character-variant"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-character-variant②"></a>

The [character-variant()](#character-variant) property value and the [@character-variant](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values-character-variant) descriptor allow two values, which enables a feature (from the first value) and sets it to a given (second) value.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-two-valued-feature"></a>
>
> ```text
> @font-feature-values MM Greek {
>       @character-variant { alpha-2: 1 2; }   /* implies cv01 = 2 */
>       @character-variant { beta-3: 2 3; }    /* implies cv02 = 3 */
>     }
> ```
<a id="ref-for-styleset"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-styleset②"></a>

For the [styleset()](#styleset) property value and [@styleset](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values-styleset) rule, multiple values indicate the style sets to be enabled. Values between 1 and 99 enable OpenType features ss01 through ss99. However, the OpenType standard only officially defines ss01 through ss20. For OpenType fonts, values greater than 99 or equal to 0 do not generate a syntax error when parsed but enable no OpenType features.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-multi-valued-feature"></a>
>
> ```text
> @font-feature-values Mars Serif {
>     @styleset {
>       alt-g: 1;        /* implies ss01 = 1 */
>       curly-quotes: 3; /* implies ss03 = 1 */
>       code: 4 5;       /* implies ss04 = 1, ss05 = 1 */
>     }
> 
>     @styleset {
>       dumb: 125;        /* >99, ignored */
>     }
> 
>     @swash {
>       swishy: 3 5;     /* more than 1 value for swash, syntax error */
>     }
>   }
> 
>   p.codeblock {
>     /* implies ss03 = 1, ss04 = 1, ss05 = 1 */
>     font-variant-alternates: styleset(curly-quotes, code);
>   }
> ```
<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-feature-values-character-variant③"></a>

For [\<@character-variant\>](#at-ruledef-font-feature-values-character-variant), a single value between 1 and 99 indicates the enabling of OpenType feature cv01 through cv99. For OpenType fonts, values greater than 99 or equal to 0 are ignored but do not generate a syntax error when parsed but enable no OpenType features. When two values are listed, the first value indicates the feature used and the second the value passed for that feature. If more than two values are assigned to a given name, a syntax error occurs and the entire feature value definition is ignored.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-greek-variant-features"></a>
>
> ```text
> @font-feature-values MM Greek {
>     @character-variant { alpha-2: 1 2; }   /* implies cv01 = 2 */
>     @character-variant { beta-3: 2 3; }    /* implies cv02 = 3 */
>     @character-variant { epsilon: 5 3 6; } /* more than 2 values, syntax error, definition ignored */
>     @character-variant { gamma: 12; }      /* implies cv12 = 1 */
>     @character-variant { zeta:   20 3; }   /* implies cv20 = 3 */
>     @character-variant { zeta-2: 20 2; }   /* implies cv20 = 2 */
>     @character-variant { silly: 105; }     /* >99, ignored */
>     @character-variant { dumb: 323 3; }    /* >99, ignored */
>   }
> 
>   #title {
>     /* use the third alternate beta, first alternate gamma */
>     font-variant-alternates: character-variant(beta-3, gamma);
>   }
> 
>   p {
>     /* zeta-2 follows zeta, implies cv20 = 2  */
>     font-variant-alternates: character-variant(zeta, zeta-2);
>   }
> 
>   .special {
>     /* zeta follows zeta-2, implies cv20 = 3  */
>     font-variant-alternates: character-variant(zeta-2, zeta);
>   }
> ```
![Matching text on Byzantine seals using character variants](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/byzantineseal.png)

Byzantine seal text displayed with character variants

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-byzantine-variants"></a>
>
> In the figure above, the text in red is rendered using a font containing character variants that mimic the character forms found on a Byzantine seal from the 8th century A.D. Two lines below is the same text displayed in a font without variants. Note the two variants for U and N used on the seal.
>
> ```text
> @font-feature-values Athena Ruby {
>       @character-variant {
>         leo-B: 2 1;
>         leo-M: 13 3;
>         leo-alt-N: 14 1;
>         leo-N: 14 2;
>         leo-T: 20 1;
>         leo-U: 21 2;
>         leo-alt-U: 21 4;
>       }
>     }
> 
>     p {
>       font-variant: discretionary-ligatures
>       character-variant(leo-B, leo-M, leo-N, leo-T, leo-U);
>     }
> 
>     span.alt-N {
>       font-variant-alternates: character-variant(leo-alt-N);
>     }
> 
>     span.alt-U {
>       font-variant-alternates: character-variant(leo-alt-U);
>     }
> 
>     <p>ENO....UP͞RSTU<span class="alt-U">U</span>͞<span class="alt-U">U</span>ΚΑΙTỤẠG̣IUPNS</p>
> 
>     <p>LEON|ΚΑΙCONSTA|NTI<span class="alt-N">N</span>OS..|STOIBAṢ.|LIṢROM|AIO<span class="alt-N">N</span></p>
> ```

Allows control of glyph substitution and sizing in East Asian text.

<a id="east-asian-variant-values"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①③④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①③⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①③⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①③⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①③⑧"></a>

```text
<east-asian-variant-values> = [ jis78 | jis83 | jis90 | jis04 | simplified | traditional ]
```
<a id="east-asian-width-values"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①③⑨"></a>

```text
<east-asian-width-values>   = [ full-width | proportional-width ]
```
Individual values have the following meanings:

<a id="font-variant-east-asian-normal-value"></a>normal  
None of the features listed below are enabled.

<a id="valdef-font-variant-east-asian-jis78"></a>jis78  
Enables rendering of JIS78 forms (OpenType feature: jp78).

![JIS78 form example](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/jp78.png)

<a id="valdef-font-variant-east-asian-jis83"></a>jis83  
Enables rendering of JIS83 forms (OpenType feature: jp83).

<a id="valdef-font-variant-east-asian-jis90"></a>jis90  
Enables rendering of JIS90 forms (OpenType feature: jp90).

<a id="valdef-font-variant-east-asian-jis04"></a>jis04  
Enables rendering of JIS2004 forms (OpenType feature: jp04).

The various JIS variants reflect the glyph forms defined in different Japanese national standards. Fonts generally include glyphs defined by the most recent national standard, but it’s sometimes necessary to use older variants, to match signage for example.

<a id="valdef-font-variant-east-asian-simplified"></a>simplified  
Enables rendering of simplified forms (OpenType feature: smpl).

<a id="valdef-font-variant-east-asian-traditional"></a>traditional  
Enables rendering of traditional forms (OpenType feature: trad).

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-variant-east-asian-simplified"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-variant-east-asian-traditional"></a>

The [simplified](#valdef-font-variant-east-asian-simplified) and [traditional](#valdef-font-variant-east-asian-traditional) values allow control over the glyph forms for characters which have been simplified over time but for which the older, traditional form is still used in some contexts. The exact set of characters and glyph forms will vary to some degree by the context for which a given font was designed.

![traditional form example](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/trad.png)

<a id="valdef-font-variant-east-asian-full-width"></a>full-width  
Enables rendering of full-width variants (OpenType feature: fwid).

<a id="valdef-font-variant-east-asian-proportional-width"></a>proportional-width  
Enables rendering of proportionally-spaced variants (OpenType feature: pwid).

![proportionally spaced Japanese example](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/pwid.png)

<a id="valdef-font-variant-east-asian-ruby"></a>ruby  
Enables display of ruby variant glyphs (OpenType feature: ruby). Since ruby text is generally smaller than the associated body text, font designers can design special glyphs for use with ruby that are more readable than scaled down versions of the default glyphs. Only glyph selection is affected, there is no associated font scaling or other change that affects line layout.

The red ruby text below is shown with default glyphs (top) and with ruby variant glyphs (bottom). Note the slight difference in stroke thickness.

![ruby variant example](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/rubyshinkansen.png)

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant①⓪"></a>

The [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) property is a shorthand for all font-variant subproperties:

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-ligatures②"></a>

  [font-variant-ligatures](#propdef-font-variant-ligatures)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-position⑧"></a>

  [font-variant-position](#propdef-font-variant-position)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-caps③"></a>

  [font-variant-caps](#propdef-font-variant-caps)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-numeric②"></a>

  [font-variant-numeric](#propdef-font-variant-numeric)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-alternates⑤"></a>

  [font-variant-alternates](#propdef-font-variant-alternates)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-east-asian②"></a>

  [font-variant-east-asian](#propdef-font-variant-east-asian)

- <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-emoji①"></a>

  [font-variant-emoji](#propdef-font-variant-emoji)

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant①①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-ligatures③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant①②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant①③"></a>

The value <a id="font-variant-normal-value"></a>normal resets all subproperties of [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) to their initial value. The <a id="font-variant-none-value"></a>none value sets [font-variant-ligatures](#propdef-font-variant-ligatures) to 'font-variant-ligatures/none' and resets all other font feature properties to their initial value. Like other shorthands, using [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) resets unspecified [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) subproperties to their initial values.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-language-override⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings③"></a>

It does not reset the values of [font-language-override](#propdef-font-language-override), [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings) or [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings).

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings⑦"></a>

This property provides low-level control over OpenType font features. It is intended as a way of providing access to font features that are not widely used but are needed for a particular use case.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings⑧"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="advisement">Advisement</strong>
>
> Authors should <em>not</em> use [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings) to set any of the font features in the table below. Instead, please use the higher-level replacement properties, because:
>
> 1.  <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings⑨"></a>
>
>     The higher-level properties cascade individually. You can set one without setting the whole [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings) list
>
> 2.  Some higher-level properties can be synthesized for fonts that do not support the font feature.
>
> <a id="ffs-replacements"></a>
>
> | If you want to set this font feature                                               | <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings①⓪"></a>then use this <em>instead of</em> [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings)                                                                       | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
