> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-scientific-inferiors"></a>
>
> For example, there is no font-variant value to control Scientific Inferiors (small numerals used in chemical formulae). Readability is enhanced by using them, so they must be enabled using font-feature-settings:
>
> ```text
> .chem {
>   font-feature-settings: 'sinf'
> }
> ```
>
> ![scientific inferior numbers in a chemical formula](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/scientific-inferiors.png)
>
> Scientific inferiors improve readability of chemical formulae

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> The entire property value is set at once. Unlike the font-variant properties, there is no way to modify the inherited value by adding or removing individual features.

A value of <a id="font-feature-settings-normal-value"></a>normal means that no change in glyph selection or positioning occurs due to this property.

Feature tag values have the following syntax:

<a id="feature-tag-value"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-comma"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑤①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑤②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-mult-opt⑥"></a>

<a id="typedef-opentype-tag"></a>

```text
<feature-tag-value> = <opentype-tag> [ <integer [0,∞]> | on | off ]?
<opentype-tag> = <string>
```
<a id="ref-for-typedef-opentype-tag"></a>

The [\<opentype-tag\>](#typedef-opentype-tag) is a case-sensitive OpenType feature tag. As specified in the OpenType specification [\[OPENTYPE\]](#biblio-opentype), feature tags contain four ASCII characters. Tag strings longer or shorter than four characters, or containing characters outside the U+20–7E codepoint range are invalid. Feature tags need only match a feature tag defined in the font, so they are not limited to explicitly registered OpenType features. Fonts defining custom feature tags should follow the [tag name rules](https://www.microsoft.com/typography/otspec/featuretags.htm) defined in the OpenType specification [\[OPENTYPE-FEATURES\]](#biblio-opentype-features).

<a id="must-not-synthesize-features"></a>Feature tags not present in the font are ignored; a user agent must not attempt to synthesize fallback behavior based on these feature tags. The one exception is that user agents may synthetically support the kern feature with fonts that contain kerning data in the form of a `kern` table but lack kern feature support in the `GPOS` table.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-kerning④"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> In general, authors should use the [font-kerning](#propdef-font-kerning) property to explicitly enable or disable kerning since this property always affects fonts with either type of kerning data.

<a id="ref-for-integer-value②"></a>

If present, a value indicates an index used for glyph selection. An [\<integer\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#integer-value) value must be 0 or greater. A value of 0 indicates that the feature is disabled. For boolean features, a value of 1 enables the feature. For non-boolean features, a value of 1 or greater enables the feature and indicates the feature selection index. A value of <a id="valdef-feature-tag-value-on"></a>on is synonymous with 1 and <a id="valdef-feature-tag-value-off"></a>off is synonymous with 0. If the value is omitted, a value of 1 is assumed.

<a id="ffs-map"></a>The computed value of font-feature-settings is a map, so any duplicates in the specified value must not be preserved. If the same feature tag appears more than once, the value associated with the last appearance supersedes any previous value for that axis.

<a id="ref-for-code-unit"></a>

<a id="ffs-sorted"></a>The computed value contains the de-duplicated feature tags, sorted in ascending order by [code unit](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-unit).

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-font-feature-settings-map"></a>
>
> ```text
> font-feature-settings: "sinf" 1;       /* sinf=1 enable Scientific Inferiors */
> font-feature-settings: "sinf" on;      /* sinf=1 enable Scientific Inferiors */
> font-feature-settings: 'sinf';         /* sinf=1 enable Scientific Inferiors */
> font-feature-settings: "sinf" off;     /* sinf=0 disable Scientific Inferiors */
> font-feature-settings: "sinf", 'twid'; /* sinf=1, twid=1 enable Scientific Inferiors and Third Widths */
> font-feature-settings: "sinf" "twid";  /* invalid, need a comma-delimited list */
> font-feature-settings: "silly" off;    /* invalid, tag too long */
> font-feature-settings: "PKRN";         /* PKRN=1 enable custom feature */
> font-feature-settings: sinf;           /* invalid, tag must be a string */
> ```
When values greater than the range supported by the font are specified, the behavior is explicitly undefined. For boolean features, in general these will enable the feature. For non-boolean features, out of range values will in general be equivalent to a 0 value. However, in both cases the exact behavior will depend upon the way the font is designed (specifically, which type of lookup is used to define the feature).

Implementations may choose to ignore turning off features which the OpenType specification says are always required, such as required ligatures "rlig".

Tests

- [font-variant-ligatures-11.optional.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-variant-ligatures-11.optional.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-variant-ligatures-11.optional.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-variant-ligatures-11.optional.html)

Although specifically defined for OpenType feature tags, feature tags for other modern font formats that support font features may be added in the future. Where possible, features defined for other font formats should attempt to follow the pattern of registered OpenType tags.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-half-width-kana"></a>
>
> The Japanese text below will be rendered with half-width kana characters:
>
> ```text
> body { font-feature-settings: "hwid"; /* Half-width OpenType feature */ }
> 
> <p>毎日カレー食べてるのに、飽きない</p>
> ```
<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-language-override⑦"></a>

### <a id="font-language-override-prop"></a>6.13. Font language override: the [font-language-override](#propdef-font-language-override) property

| Field               | Definition                                                                                                                                                     |
|---------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="propdef-font-language-override"></a>font-language-override                                                                                                                      |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#value-defs">Value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-string-value⑦"></a><a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑤③"></a>normal [\|](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#comb-one) [\<string\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#string-value) |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#initial-values">Initial:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | normal                                                                                                                                                         |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#applies-to">Applies to:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | all elements and text                                                                                                                                          |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#inherited-property">Inherited:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | yes                                                                                                                                                            |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#percentages">Percentages:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | N/A                                                                                                                                                            |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#computed">Computed value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | specified string or the keyword none                                                                                                                           |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/cssom/#serializing-css-values">Canonical order:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | per grammar                                                                                                                                                    |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/web-animations/#animation-type">Animation type:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | discrete                                                                                                                                                       |

Tests

- [font-language-override-01.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-language-override-01.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-language-override-01.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-language-override-01.html)
- [font-language-override-02.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-language-override-02.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-language-override-02.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-language-override-02.html)
- [font-language-override-03.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-language-override-03.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-language-override-03.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-language-override-03.html)
- [font-language-override-computed.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-language-override-computed.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-language-override-computed.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-language-override-computed.html)
- [font-language-override-invalid.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-language-override-invalid.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-language-override-invalid.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-language-override-invalid.html)
- [font-language-override-valid.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-language-override-valid.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-language-override-valid.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-language-override-valid.html)

Normally, authors can control the use of language-specific glyph substitutions and positioning by setting the content language of an element, as [described above](#language-specific-support):

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-font-lang-override"></a>
>
> ```text
> <!-- Display text using S'gaw Karen specific features -->
> 
>     <p lang="ksw">...</p>
> ```
<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-language-override⑧"></a>

In some cases, authors may need to specify a language system that differs from the content language, for example due to the need to mimic another language’s typographic traditions. The [font-language-override](#propdef-font-language-override) property allows authors to explicitly specify the language system of the font, overriding the language system implied by the content language.

Values have the following meanings:

<a id="font-language-override-normal-value"></a>normal

specifies that when rendering with OpenType fonts, the content language of the element is used to infer the OpenType language system

<a id="ref-for-string-value⑧"></a>

<a id="font-language-override-string-value"></a>[\<string\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#string-value)

single four-character case-sensitive OpenType [language system tag](https://www.microsoft.com/typography/otspec/languagetags.htm), specifies the OpenType language system to be used instead of the language system implied by the language of the element. If the string is shorter than four characters, it is padded at the end with space (U+0020) characters such that the length is 4, before being matched.

Unknown OpenType language system tags are silently ignored, and do not affect glyph selection and placement.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-related-language"></a> The [Universal Declaration of Human Rights](https://www.un.org/en/about-us/universal-declaration-of-human-rights) has been translated into a wide variety of languages. In Turkish, Article 9 of this document might be marked up as below:
>
> ```text
> <body lang="tr">
>     <h4>Madde 9</h4>
>     <p>Hiç kimse keyfi olarak tutuklanamaz, alıkonulanamaz veya sürülemez.</p>
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-language-override⑨"></a>
>
> Here the user agent uses the value of the `lang` attribute when rendering text and appropriately renders this text without 'fi' ligatures. There is no need to use the [font-language-override](#propdef-font-language-override) property.
>
> However, a given font may lack support for a specific language. In this situation authors may need to use the typographic conventions of a related language that are supported by that font:
>
> ```text
> <body lang="mk">     <!-- Macedonian lang code -->
>     body { font-language-override: "SRB"; /* Serbian OpenType language tag */ }
> 
>     <h4>Члeн 9</h4>
>     <p>Никoj чoвeк нeмa дa бидe пoдлoжeн нa прoизвoлнo aпсeњe, притвoр или прoгoнувaњe.</p>
> ```
>
> As the content creator knows that font specified supports Serbian, the Macedonian text here will be rendered using Serbian typographic conventions.

## <a id="font-feature-variation-resolution"></a>7.  Font Feature and Variation Resolution

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant①④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings①①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑥③"></a>

As described in the previous section, font features and variations can be enabled in a variety of ways, either via the use of [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant), [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings), or [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings) in a style rule or within an [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule. The resolution order for the union of these settings is defined below. Features defined via CSS properties are applied on top of layout engine default features.

### <a id="default-features"></a>7.1.  Default features

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant①⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings①②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-ligatures⑧"></a>

For OpenType fonts, user agents must enable the default features defined in the OpenType documentation for a given script and writing mode. Required ligatures, common ligatures and contextual forms must be enabled by default (OpenType features: rlig, liga, clig, calt), along with localized forms (OpenType feature: locl), and features required for proper display of composed characters and marks (OpenType features: ccmp, mark, mkmk). These features must always be enabled, even when the value of the [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) and [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings) properties is normal. Individual features are only disabled when explicitly overridden by the author, as when [font-variant-ligatures](#propdef-font-variant-ligatures) is set to 'font-variant-ligatures/no-common-ligatures'.

Tests

- [font-default-01.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-default-01.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-default-01.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-default-01.html)
- [font-default-02.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-default-02.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-default-02.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-default-02.html)
- [font-default-03.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-default-03.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-default-03.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-default-03.html)
- [font-default-04.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-default-04.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-default-04.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-default-04.html)

For handling complex scripts such as [Arabic](https://www.microsoft.com/typography/otfntdev/arabicot/features.aspx), [Mongolian](https://www.microsoft.com/typography/otfntdev/mongolot/features.htm) or [Devanagari](https://www.microsoft.com/typography/otfntdev/devanot/features.aspx) additional features are required. For upright text within vertical text runs, vertical alternates (OpenType feature: vert) must be enabled.

### <a id="feature-variation-precedence"></a>7.2.  Feature and variation precedence

<a id="ref-for-font-specific⑧"></a>

General and [font specific](#font-specific) font feature property settings are resolved in the order below, in ascending order of precedence. This ordering is used to construct a combined list of font features that affect a given text run.

1.  Font features enabled by default are applied, including features required for a given script. See [§ 7.1 Default features](#default-features) for a description of these.

2.  <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight②④"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width②③"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style②④"></a>

    <a id="apply-font-matching-variations"></a>Font variations as enabled by the [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight), [font-width](#propdef-font-width), and [font-style](#propdef-font-style) properties are applied.

    <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style②⑤"></a>

    The application of the value enabled by [font-style](#propdef-font-style) is affected by font selection, because this property might select an italic or an oblique font. The value applied is the closest matching value as determined by the [font matching algorithm](#font-style-matching). User agents must apply at most one value due to the <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style②⑥"></a>font-style property; both "ital" and "slnt" values must not be set together.

    <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑥④"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-weight①"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-width②"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-style②"></a>

    If the selected font is defined in an [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule, then the values applied at this step should be clamped to the value of the [font-weight](#descdef-font-face-font-weight), [font-width](#descdef-font-face-font-width), and [font-style](#descdef-font-face-font-style) descriptors in that <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑥⑤"></a>@font-face rule.

    Then, the values applied in this step should be clamped (possibly again) to the values that are supported by the font.

3.  The language specified by the inherited value of lang/xml:lang is applied.

4.  <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑥⑥"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-language-override①"></a>

    If the font is defined via an [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule, the font language override implied by the [font-language-override](#descdef-font-face-font-language-override) descriptor in the <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑥⑦"></a>@font-face rule is applied.

5.  <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑥⑧"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-named-instance③"></a>

    If the font is defined via an [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule, that <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑥⑨"></a>@font-face rule includes at least one valid [font-named-instance](#descdef-font-face-font-named-instance) descriptor with a value other than 'font-named-instance/none', and the loaded font resource includes a named instance with that name according to the [§ 5.1 Localized name matching](#localized-name-matching) rules, then all the variation values represented by that named instance are applied. These values are clamped to the values that are supported by the font.

6.  <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑦⓪"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-variation-settings①"></a>

    If the font is defined via an [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule, the font variations implied by the [font-variation-settings](#descdef-font-face-font-variation-settings) descriptor in the <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑦①"></a>@font-face rule are applied.

7.  <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑦②"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-font-feature-settings①"></a>

    If the font is defined via an [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule, the font features implied by the [font-feature-settings](#descdef-font-face-font-feature-settings) descriptor in the <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑦③"></a>@font-face rule are applied.

8.  <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-language-override①⓪"></a>

    The font language override implied by the value of the [font-language-override](#propdef-font-language-override) property is applied.

9.  <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-optical-sizing②"></a>

    Font variations implied by the value of the [font-optical-sizing](#propdef-font-optical-sizing) property are applied.

10. <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant①⑥"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-kerning⑤"></a>

    Font features implied by the value of the [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) property, the related <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant①⑦"></a>font-variant subproperties and any other CSS property that uses OpenType features (e.g. the [font-kerning](#propdef-font-kerning) property) are applied.

11. <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant①⑧"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings①③"></a>

    <a id="ref-for-propdef-letter-spacing①"></a>

    Feature settings determined by properties other than [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) or [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings) are applied. For example, setting a non-default value for the [letter-spacing](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-text-4/#propdef-letter-spacing) property disables optional ligatures.

12. <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings⑤"></a>

    Font variations implied by the value of the [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings) property are applied. These values should be clamped to the values that are supported by the font.

13. <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings①④"></a>

    Font features implied by the value of [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings) property are applied.

Tests

- [font-feature-resolution-001.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-feature-resolution-001.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-feature-resolution-001.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-feature-resolution-001.html)
- [font-feature-resolution-002.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-feature-resolution-002.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-feature-resolution-002.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-feature-resolution-002.html)
- [lang-attribute-affects-rendering.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/lang-attribute-affects-rendering.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/lang-attribute-affects-rendering.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/lang-attribute-affects-rendering.html)
- [lang-attribute-affects-rendering-of-second-text-run.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/lang-attribute-affects-rendering-of-second-text-run.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/lang-attribute-affects-rendering-of-second-text-run.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/lang-attribute-affects-rendering-of-second-text-run.html)

<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑦④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant①⑨"></a>

This ordering allows authors to set up a general set of defaults for fonts within their [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rules, then override them with property settings for specific elements. General property settings override the settings in <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑦⑤"></a>@font-face rules and low-level font feature settings override [font-variant](#propdef-font-variant) property settings.

For situations where the combined list of font feature settings contains more than one value for the same feature, the last value is used. When a font lacks support for a given underlying font feature, text is simply rendered as if that font feature was not enabled; font fallback does not occur and no attempt is made to synthesize the feature except where explicitly defined for specific properties.

### <a id="feature-precedence-examples"></a>7.3.  Feature precedence examples

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-tabular-prices"></a> With the styles below, numbers are rendered proportionally when used within a paragraph but are shown in tabular form within tables of prices:
>
> ```text
> body {
>   font-variant-numeric: proportional-nums;
> }
> 
> table.prices td {
>   font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
> }
> ```
<a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑦⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-descdef-font-face-src⑨"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-features-local"></a> The [@font-face](#at-font-face-rule) rule can also be used to access font features in locally available fonts via the use of `local()` in the [src](#descdef-font-face-src) descriptor of the <a id="ref-for-at-font-face-rule⑦⑦"></a>@font-face definition:
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: BodyText;
>   src: local("HiraMaruPro-W4");
>   font-variant: proportional-width;
>   font-feature-settings: "ital"; /* Latin italics within CJK text feature */
> }
> 
> body { font-family: BodyText, serif; }
> ```
>
> If available, a Japanese font "Hiragino Maru Gothic" will be used. When text rendering occurs, Japanese kana will be proportionally spaced and Latin text will be italicized. Text rendered with the fallback serif font will use default rendering properties.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-no-dlig-in-special"></a> In the example below, discretionary ligatures are enabled only for a downloadable font but are disabled within spans of class "special":
>
> ```text
> @font-face {
>   font-family: main;
>   src: url(fonts/ffmeta.woff) format("woff");
>   font-variant: discretionary-ligatures;
> }
> 
> body         { font-family: main, Helvetica; }
> span.special { font-variant-ligatures: no-discretionary-ligatures; }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings①⑤"></a>
>
> Suppose one adds a rule using [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings) to enable discretionary ligatures:
>
> ```text
> body         { font-family: main, Helvetica; }
> span         { font-feature-settings: "dlig"; }
> span.special { font-variant-ligatures: no-discretionary-ligatures; }
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings①⑥"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-ligatures⑨"></a>
>
> <a id="ref-for-valdef-font-variant-ligatures-no-discretionary-ligatures"></a>
>
> In this case, discretionary ligatures <em>will</em> be rendered within spans of class "special". This is because both the [font-feature-settings](#propdef-font-feature-settings) and [font-variant-ligatures](#propdef-font-variant-ligatures) properties apply to these spans. Although the [no-discretionary-ligatures](#valdef-font-variant-ligatures-no-discretionary-ligatures) setting of <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variant-ligatures①⓪"></a>font-variant-ligatures effectively disables the OpenType "dlig" feature, because the <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-feature-settings①⑦"></a>font-feature-settings is resolved after that, the "dlig" value reenables discretionary ligatures.

## <a id="font-variation-props"></a>8.  Font Variation Properties

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: The technology in use in this section is named "font variations." An instance of one such font as a "variable font."

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-optical-sizing③"></a>

### <a id="font-optical-sizing-def"></a>8.1.  Optical sizing control: the [font-optical-sizing](#propdef-font-optical-sizing) property

| Field               | Definition                                                                      |
|---------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="propdef-font-optical-sizing"></a>font-optical-sizing                                          |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#value-defs">Value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑤④"></a>auto [\|](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#comb-one) none |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#initial-values">Initial:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | auto                                                                            |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#applies-to">Applies to:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | all elements and text                                                           |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#inherited-property">Inherited:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | yes                                                                             |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#percentages">Percentages:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | n/a                                                                             |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#computed">Computed value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | specified keyword                                                               |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/cssom/#serializing-css-values">Canonical order:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | per grammar                                                                     |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/web-animations/#animation-type">Animation type:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | discrete                                                                        |

Tests

- [font-optical-sizing-computed.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-optical-sizing-computed.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-optical-sizing-computed.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-optical-sizing-computed.html)
- [font-optical-sizing-invalid.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-optical-sizing-invalid.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-optical-sizing-invalid.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-optical-sizing-invalid.html)
- [font-optical-sizing-valid.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-optical-sizing-valid.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-optical-sizing-valid.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/parsing/font-optical-sizing-valid.html)

Typographically, text rendered at different sizes often benefits from slightly different visual representations. For example, to aid reading at small text sizes, strokes are often thicker with larger serifs. Larger text often has a more delicate figure with more contrast between thicker and thinner strokes.

<a id="font-optical-sizing-auto-value"></a>auto  
The user agent may modify the shape of glyphs based on the font-size and the pixel density of the screen. For OpenType and TrueType fonts using font variations, this is often done by using the "opsz" font variation.

<a id="font-optical-sizing-none-value"></a>none  
The user agent must not modify the shape of glyphs for optical size.

<a id="optical-size-example"></a> ![text with different optical sizes, normalized to similar physical sizes](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/Century_Expanded-a-normalized.jpg)

Each size of Century Expanded as it existed in analog metal form. The different optical sizes, normalized here to the same physical size, have design variations to maintain stylistic traits and improve readability.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size①⑨"></a>

[font-size](#propdef-font-size) must be considered when selecting a variation value for the "opsz" axis, but other signals may also be considered.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size②⓪"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: User agents are expected to supply a value for the "opsz" axis which is close to the used value for [font-size](#propdef-font-size). However, user agents might wish to consider other factors such as pixel density of the screen, or the solid angle the text subtends in the viewer’s retina. [Experiments have shown](https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/807#issue-195683688), however, that disregarding these other ancillary factors and using only <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size②①"></a>font-size might be the best way for a user agent to select this value.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-optical-sizing④"></a>

Pixel density as well as visual size of the text may influence the variation value chosen for [font-optical-sizing](#propdef-font-optical-sizing). When either pixel density or visual size of the text changes in response to a user operation or style change, user agents must not choose a new value for this variation value unless the change is layout-causing. User agents are free to determine which changes are layout-causing.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-transform"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: Some user operations, such as pinch-zoom, can be considered not-layout-causing if they do not cause text to reflow. However, other user operations, such as increasing text size for accessibility purposes, can be considered layout-causing because they cause text to reflow. Similarly, the [transform](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-transforms-1/#propdef-transform) property can be considered not-layout-causing because transforms generally do not cause text to reflow. Each user-agent is free to decide whether or not each operation is layout-changing or not.

User agents must not synthesize optical sizing when it is not performed by the font directly.

User agents must not select a value for the "opsz" axis which is not supported by the font used for rendering the text. This can be accomplished by clamping a chosen value to the range supported by the font.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-optical-sizing⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-size-adjust①⑥"></a>

[font-optical-sizing](#propdef-font-optical-sizing) interacts with [font-size-adjust](#propdef-font-size-adjust). When applying optical sizing, User agents must apply an optical sizing value appropriate for the adjusted font size, subject to the above restrictions.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings⑥"></a>

### <a id="font-variation-settings-def"></a>8.2.  Low-level font variation settings control: the [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings) property

| Field               | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
|---------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="propdef-font-variation-settings"></a>font-variation-settings                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#value-defs">Value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-mult-comma①②"></a><a id="ref-for-number-value⑤"></a><a id="ref-for-typedef-opentype-tag①"></a><a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑤⑤"></a>normal [\|](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#comb-one) \[ [\<opentype-tag\>](#typedef-opentype-tag) [\<number\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#number-value) \][\#](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#mult-comma) |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#initial-values">Initial:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | normal                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#applies-to">Applies to:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | all elements and text                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#inherited-property">Inherited:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | yes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#percentages">Percentages:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | n/a                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#computed">Computed value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | the keyword normal or a list, each item a string paired with a number                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/cssom/#serializing-css-values">Canonical order:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | per grammar                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/web-animations/#animation-type">Animation type:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | see prose                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

Tests

- [font-variation-settings-calc.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-variation-settings-calc.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-variation-settings-calc.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-variation-settings-calc.html)
- [variable-in-font-variation-settings.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/variable-in-font-variation-settings.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/variable-in-font-variation-settings.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/variable-in-font-variation-settings.html)
- [variable-box-font.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-box-font.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-box-font.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-box-font.html)
- [variable-gpos-m2b.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-gpos-m2b.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-gpos-m2b.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-gpos-m2b.html)
- [variable-gsub.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-gsub.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-gsub.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-gsub.html)
- [variable-opsz-size-adjust.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-opsz-size-adjust.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-opsz-size-adjust.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-opsz-size-adjust.html)
- [variable-opsz.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-opsz.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-opsz.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/variations/variable-opsz.html)

This property provides low-level control over OpenType or TrueType font variations. It is intended as a way of providing access to font variations that are not widely used but are needed for a particular use case.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings⑦"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="advisement">Advisement</strong>
>
> Authors should <em>not</em> use [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings) to set any of the variation axes in the table below. Instead, please use the higher-level replacement properties, because:
>
> 1.  <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings⑧"></a>
>
>     The higher-level properties cascade individually. You can set one without setting the whole [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings) list
>
> 2.  Some higher-level properties can be synthesized for fonts that do not support the font variation.
>
> <a id="fvs-replacements"></a>
>
> | If you want to set this variation axis                  | <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings⑨"></a>then use this <em>instead of</em> [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings) | Notes                                                                                                                                               |
> |---------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
> | Weight (<code>wght</code>)                              | <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight②⑤"></a>[font-weight](#propdef-font-weight)                                                           | <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight②⑥"></a>The [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight) property will set the <code>wght</code> axis if one is present.                          |
> | Width (<code>wdth</code>)                               | <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width②④"></a>[font-width](#propdef-font-width)                                                             | <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-width②⑤"></a>The [font-width](#propdef-font-width) property will set the <code>wdth</code> axis if one is present.                            |
> | Slant (<code>slnt</code>) or Italic (<code>ital</code>) | <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style②⑦"></a>[font-style](#propdef-font-style)                                                             | <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style②⑧"></a>The [font-style](#propdef-font-style) property will set the <code>slnt</code> or <code>ital</code> axis, depending on its value. |
> | Optical size (<code>opsz</code>)                        | <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-optical-sizing⑥"></a>[font-optical-sizing](#propdef-font-optical-sizing)                                           | <a id="ref-for-propdef-font-optical-sizing⑦"></a>The [font-optical-sizing](#propdef-font-optical-sizing) property will set the <code>opsz</code> axis if one is present.          |

The use of font variation settings does not affect font selection where a fallback font is to be used, where using font properties for the same axis would have an effect on font selection.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-optical-sizing⑧"></a>

When possible, authors should generally use the other properties related to font variations (such as [font-optical-sizing](#propdef-font-optical-sizing)), and only use this property for special cases where its use is the only way of accessing a particular infrequently used font variation.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-weight②⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings①⓪"></a>

For example, it is preferable to use [font-weight](#propdef-font-weight): 700 rather than [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings): "wght" 700.

A value of normal means that no change in glyph shape, matching, or positioning occurs due to this property.

<a id="ref-for-typedef-opentype-tag②"></a>

The [\<opentype-tag\>](#typedef-opentype-tag) is a case-sensitive OpenType or TrueType variation axis name. As specified in the OpenType / TrueType specifications, axis names contain four ASCII characters. Axis name strings longer or shorter than four characters, or containing characters outside the U+20–7E codepoint range are invalid. Axis names need only match an axis tag defined in the font, so they are not limited to explicitly registered OpenType / TrueType variation axes. Fonts defining custom axis names should follow the [name rules](https://www.microsoft.com/typography/otspec/fvar.htm) defined in the OpenType specification.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings①①"></a>

If a given axis is not supported by a font, its value setting is ignored and therefore has no effect; a user agent must not attempt to synthesize fallback behavior based on these axis tags. Other axis values within the same CSS [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings) statement are not ignored.

If a variation axis is supported by a font but its set value is greater or less than the predefined font range, it will be clamped to the closest value supported by the font.

Values are allowed to be fractional or negative.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-style②⑨"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-variation-settings①②"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: it is preferable to obtain slanted faces by using the [font-style](#propdef-font-style) property. However, if using the `slnt` axis directly, via [font-variation-settings](#propdef-font-variation-settings), remember that it is defined with a positive angle meaning a counter-clockwise slant, the opposite direction to CSS.

If the same axis name appears more than once, the value associated with the last appearance supersedes any previous value for that axis. This deduplication is observable by accessing the computed value of this property.

<a id="ref-for-code-unit①"></a>

<a id="fvs-sorted"></a>The computed value contains the de-duplicated axis names, sorted in ascending order by [code unit](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-unit).

Although specifically defined for OpenType / TrueType variations, variation axes for other modern font formats that support font variations might be added in the future. Where possible, variations defined for other font formats should attempt to follow the pattern of registered variation axes.

Animating font-variation-settings is possible using the following mechanism. Two declarations of font-feature-settings can be animated between if they are "like". "Like" declarations are ones where the same set of properties appear (in any order). Because successive duplicate properties are applied instead of prior duplicate properties, two declarations can be "like" even if they have differing number of properties. If two declarations are "like" then animation occurs pairwise between corresponding values in the declarations. Otherwise, animation is not possible. In this situation, the "from" values of the animation are swapped to the "to" values at an unspecified time during the animation.

## <a id="color-font-support"></a>9.  Color Font Support

Color fonts allow for font files to describe, not just the contours describing the edges of glyphs, but also the colors present inside the glyphs.

<a id="multicolore"></a> ![an example multicolored font](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/multicolore.png)

Multicolore, by Ivan Filipov

<a id="magicalunicorn"></a> ![an example multicolored font](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/please.png)

Magical Unicorn Neue Pro, by Arthur Reinders Folmer

<a id="reemkufi"></a> ![an example multicolored font](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/Reem-Kufi.png)

Reem Kufi Ink, by Khaled Hosny and Santiago Orozco

For some uses, such as emoji, having the colors fixed in the font is appropriate. For others, there is a need to control the colors used from a stylesheet.

Tests

- [font-colorization.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/font-colorization.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/font-colorization.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/font-colorization.html)

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-palette①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-palette-values"></a>

CSS provides two ways to do this. The [font-palette](#propdef-font-palette) property allows selecting one of several different palettes contained in the font. The [@font-palette-values](#at-ruledef-font-palette-values) rule allows overriding one or more colors from within a palette or even the creation of an entirely different palette.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-palette②"></a>

### <a id="font-palette-prop"></a>9.1.  Controlling Color Font Palettes: The [font-palette](#propdef-font-palette) property

Many color font file formats allow colors within glyphs to be parameterized. In these fonts, colors are referenced by index when describing the geometry of each glyph. These indices are resolved within a current active palette using a lookup table present inside the font. However, many fonts contain multiple palettes, each containing a set of complementary colors chosen by the font designer to provide pleasing visual results.

In addition, some color font file formats provide a regular, uncolored glyph outline as well as the colored versions.

| Field               | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|---------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="propdef-font-palette"></a>font-palette                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#value-defs">Value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-funcdef-palette-mix"></a><a id="ref-for-typedef-font-palette-palette-identifier"></a><a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑤⑥"></a>normal [\|](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#comb-one) light <a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑤⑦"></a>\| dark <a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑤⑧"></a>\| [\<palette-identifier\>](#typedef-font-palette-palette-identifier) <a id="ref-for-comb-one①⑤⑨"></a>\| [\<palette-mix()\>](#funcdef-palette-mix) |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#initial-values">Initial:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | normal                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#applies-to">Applies to:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | all elements and text                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#inherited-property">Inherited:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | yes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#percentages">Percentages:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | N/a                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#computed">Computed value:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | <a id="ref-for-funcdef-palette-mix①"></a>specified keyword, identifier or [\<palette-mix()\>](#funcdef-palette-mix) function. <a id="ref-for-funcdef-palette-mix②"></a>\<palette-mix()\> must be simplified to a single keyword or identifier if resulting palette is equivalent.                                                                        |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/cssom/#serializing-css-values">Canonical order:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | per grammar                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| <strong><a href="https://www.w3.org/TR/web-animations/#animation-type">Animation type:</a>&#xA;      </strong> | by computed value                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

With the <a id="funcdef-palette-mix"></a>palette-mix() function defined as follows:

<a id="ref-for-funcdef-palette-mix③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-color-interpolation-method"></a>

<a id="ref-for-mult-opt⑦"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-comma①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-palette③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-comb-all"></a>

<a id="ref-for-percentage-value⑥"></a>

<a id="ref-for-mult-opt⑧"></a>

<a id="ref-for-mult-comma①③"></a>

```text
<palette-mix()> = palette-mix( <color-interpolation-method>? ,
    [ <'font-palette'> && <percentage [0,100]>? ]# )
```
<a id="valdef-font-palette-normal"></a>normal

<a id="ref-for-valdef-color-scheme-normal"></a>

<a id="ref-for-propdef-color-scheme"></a>

If the [color-scheme](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-adjust-1/#propdef-color-scheme) property is set to a value other than [normal](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-adjust-1/#valdef-color-scheme-normal)', user-agents should select the first light or dark palette as appropriate. Otherwise, user-agents display the color font with the default palette or default glyph colorisation.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-color"></a>

User-agents should take the computed value of the [color](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-4/#propdef-color) property into consideration when a color font format requires use of the foreground color

<a id="ref-for-propdef-color①"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-FFFF-means-color"></a>In the `COLR` [\[OPENTYPE\]](#biblio-opentype) table, color index 0xFFFF should be rendered according the [color](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-4/#propdef-color) property.

<a id="ref-for-propdef-font-palette④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-palette-normal"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-normal-is-index-0"></a>For `COLR`/`CPAL` [\[OPENTYPE\]](#biblio-opentype) fonts, [font-palette](#propdef-font-palette): [normal](#valdef-font-palette-normal) usually means rendering the font with the palette in the font at index 0.

<a id="valdef-font-palette-light"></a>light

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-palette-normal①"></a>

Some color font formats include metadata marking certain palettes as applicable on a light (close to white) background. This keyword causes the user-agent to use the first available palette in the font file marked this way. If the font file format does not account for this metadata, or no palette in the font is marked this way, this value behaves as [normal](#valdef-font-palette-normal).

<a id="valdef-font-palette-dark"></a>dark

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-palette-normal②"></a>

Some color font formats include metadata marking certain palettes as applicable on a dark (close to black) background. This keyword causes the user-agent to use the first available palette in the font file marked this way. If the font file format does not account for this metadata, or no palette in the font is marked this way, this value behaves as [normal](#valdef-font-palette-normal).

<a id="ref-for-typedef-font-palette-palette-identifier①"></a>

<a id="typedef-font-palette-palette-identifier"></a>[\<palette-identifier\>](#typedef-font-palette-palette-identifier)

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-palette-normal③"></a>

<a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-palette-values①"></a>

This value identifies an CSS-defined palette to use. Users can define a palette by using the [@font-palette-values](#at-ruledef-font-palette-values) rule. If no applicable <a id="ref-for-at-ruledef-font-palette-values②"></a>@font-palette-values rule is present, this value behaves as [normal](#valdef-font-palette-normal).

<a id="ref-for-typedef-font-palette-palette-identifier②"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-dashed-ident"></a>

[\<palette-identifier\>](#typedef-font-palette-palette-identifier) is parsed as a [\<dashed-ident\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values-4/#typedef-dashed-ident).

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: Such a CSS-defined palette may be a reference to an existing palette already present in the font, be a modification of an existing palette already present in the font, or be an entirely new palette.

<a id="ref-for-funcdef-palette-mix④"></a>

<a id="typedef-font-palette-palette-mix"></a>[\<palette-mix()\>](#funcdef-palette-mix)

<a id="ref-for-funcdef-palette-mix⑤"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-palette-dark"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-palette-light"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-palette-normal④"></a>

<a id="ref-for-typedef-font-palette-palette-identifier③"></a>

This value defines a position in the interpolation between <strong>one or more</strong> palette values, identified by [\<palette-identifier\>](#typedef-font-palette-palette-identifier), by the palette keywords [normal](#valdef-font-palette-normal), [light](#valdef-font-palette-light), [dark](#valdef-font-palette-dark), or by another [\<palette-mix()\>](#funcdef-palette-mix) value.

<a id="ref-for-funcdef-palette-mix⑥"></a>

The glyphs of the font must be rendered with a palette that is created by interpolating, per [CSS Color 4 §  13. Color Interpolation](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-4/#interpolation), for each palette color index, between the matching color indices of the list of palettes specified as arguments, recursively resolving [\<palette-mix()\>](#funcdef-palette-mix) functions if needed.

<a id="ref-for-funcdef-color-mix"></a>

For each palette color, the interpolation follows the rules of the [\<color-mix()\>](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-5/#funcdef-color-mix) function.

Percentages are normalized according to [CSS Color 5 § 3.2 Percentage Normalization](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-5/#color-mix-percent-norm).

<a id="ref-for-funcdef-color-mix①"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: although it is a pointless no-op, the meaning is well defined and thus, as with [color-mix()](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-5/#funcdef-color-mix), a list size of 1 is permitted and the result is that single palette, unchanged.

Tests

- [palette-mix-computed.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-fonts/palette-mix-computed.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-fonts/palette-mix-computed.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-fonts/palette-mix-computed.html)

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-palette-light①"></a>

<a id="ref-for-valdef-font-palette-dark①"></a>

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="note">Note</strong>
>
> Note: The names [light](#valdef-font-palette-light)' and [dark](#valdef-font-palette-dark) describe the <i>background</i> the color palette is usable on, not the colors in the color palette themselves.

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-dark-mode-palette"></a> When dark mode is in effect, use a dark mode palette
>
> ```text
> @media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) {
>         .banner {font-palette: dark;
>       }
>     }
> ```
> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-palette-mix-3"></a> A font has a dark mode palette with the following three entries:
>
> 1.   \#eee8aa
> 2.   \#dd4541
> 3.   \#72342e
>
> In addition, there are two custom palettes defined, one called "--warm":
>
> 1.   oklch(85% 0.14 88)
> 2.   oklch(62% 0.13 28)
> 3.   oklch(32% 0.05 29.6)
>
> and one called "--soft":
>
> 1.   color(display-p3 0.89 0.89 0.76)
> 2.   color(display-p3 0.75 0.55 0.55)
> 3.   color(display-p3 0.508 0.435 0.422)
>
> this palette mix:
>
> ```css
> font-palette: palette-mix(dark 20%, --warm 45%, --soft);
> ```
>
> <a id="ref-for-valdef-oklab-oklab"></a>
>
> will create the following palette (the percentage of --soft is omitted, so it is 35%, and the color interpolation method is omitted, so it is [oklab](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-4/#valdef-oklab-oklab)):
>
> 1.   oklab(88.389% -0.00651 0.09474)
> 2.   oklab(64.379% 0.11148 0.05236)
> 3.   oklab(42.124% 0.04469 0.02408)

> <strong data-conversion-semantic="example">Example</strong>
>
> <a id="ex-bixa-palette-anim"></a> Animate between palettes.
>
> ```text
> @font-palette-values --pink {
>   font-family: Nabla;
>   base-palette: 1;
> }
> 
> @font-palette-values --yellow {
>   font-family: Nabla;
>   base-palette: 7;
> }
> 
> @keyframes animate-palette {
>   from {
>     font-palette: --yellow;
>   }
>   to {
>     font-palette: --pink;
>   }
> }
> 
> p {
>   font-family: Nabla;
>   animation: animate-palette 1.4s infinite alternate linear;
> }
> ```
>
> ![The word Animate animated between a pink and a yellow palette.](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-css-fonts-4-20260907/images/nabla-animated.webp)
>
> Nabla Color by typearture.com - animating between palettes 1 and 7 in the font.
